About The Stat Line

The Stat Line is an independent sports and culture desk. One stop for sports, gaming, entertainment & collectibles — built as an autoblog: stories are spotted as they happen, verified, and published automatically. No waiting on a news cycle.

Every story on this site is verified real before it goes up. Stats get re-checked against the box score, historical claims get the full year-by-year treatment, and news gets corroborated — false alarms get killed quietly. You’ll never see them.

TSL Bets — daily best bets and parlays, by Matt. Good picks only.

The Stat Line Podcast — Mack, Red, and Anastasia break down the week’s biggest stories, with the bets and the reasoning behind every pick. New episodes twice a week, on Podbean.

Sports: rare-stat alerts as they happen across the NHL, MLB, NFL, NBA and UFC, plus a morning recap of everything that mattered overnight.
Gaming, Entertainment, Collectibles: the latest news and stories from each world, same verification standard.

Collectibles · Nov 16, 2026 · 9:07 PM ET

Extra Life Secret Lair is on sale today

By Matt

Rise of the Polygons, Magic's Extra Life 2026 Secret Lair, is on sale November 16–30 at $39.99 non-foil and $49.99 foil — five low-poly cards, with half of every purchase going to Seattle Children's Hospital.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The drop

Magic: The Gathering's annual Extra Life charity drop is live. Rise of the Polygons | Extra Life 2026 is on sale at MagicSecretLair.com from November 16 through November 30, closing at 1 a.m. PT on the 30th. The five-card set costs $39.99 non-foil and $49.99 foil — the same prices as the 2024 Extra Life drop.

Half of every purchase price, less third-party transaction fees, goes to Children's Miracle Network via Our Change Foundation, supporting Extra Life and Seattle Children's Hospital. On a $39.99 non-foil order, that's about $20 to the cause. This year's money is earmarked for mental and behavioral health care at Seattle Children's.

What's in the box

The drop is built like a lost N64 cartridge. All five pieces are illustrated by Brendan Sullivan in a late-1990s low-poly 3D video game style, and they tell a little story: the Young Pyromancer levels up through a treasure room and a legendary sword before the final boss, the Balefire Dragon.

Balefire Dragon: Mythic

Sword of Truth and Justice: Mythic

Big Score: Rare

Young Pyromancer: Rare

Elemental token (1/1 red): Token

The 2024 Extra Life drop went back to the 16-bit era. This year Wizards jumped a console generation forward, to blocky polygons and a charming sidekick with advice on how to beat that one especially tricky puzzle. The scenes were later revealed to be actual frames from a short film.

The charity behind it

Extra Life is the fundraising program where gamers raise money for Children's Miracle Network Hospitals, usually by streaming or hosting marathon game sessions and collecting donations. Wizards of the Coast has partnered with Extra Life since 2013, and at MagicCon: Atlanta this past weekend the company announced Magic players have now raised $10 million for Seattle Children's through the partnership.

Limited copies of Rise of the Polygons were sold early at the MagicCon: Atlanta merch booth from November 13 to 15 — at the convention, 100% of purchases went to Extra Life. The online sale runs at the standard 50% split.

Also on MTG Arena

The fictional video game from the drop crosses over into the real one: two bundles and a special emote are available in the MTG Arena store from November 16 to 30, including an Extra Life Secret Lair Sleeve Bundle priced at 3,000 gems with five sleeves based on the drop's art.

NHL · Nov 16, 2026 · 9:11 AM ET

Celebrini's three points end Nashville's seven-game streak

By Matt

Macklin Celebrini had a goal and two assists as San Jose beat Nashville 4-2 on Sunday — his 26th career three-point game, tying Dale Hawerchuk for fifth most in NHL history before age 21.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Macklin Celebrini had a goal and two assists as the San Jose Sharks beat the Nashville Predators 4-2 at SAP Center on Sunday, ending Nashville's seven-game winning streak. It was his 26th career three-point game, tying Dale Hawerchuk for the fifth most in NHL history before age 21.

Will Smith matched him point for point — a goal and two assists of his own — and it was the eighth time the two have each posted three points in the same game. Ivar Stenberg added a goal and an assist, and the Sharks never trailed.

How it happened

Smith opened the scoring 12:25 into the first period, finishing a breakaway set up by Stenberg and Celebrini. Collin Graf made it 2-0 late in the period, and Stenberg buried the winner at 4:45 of the second — a tap-in at the left post off a saucer pass from Smith after Juuse Saros lost his stick.

Nils Hoglander got Nashville on the board late in the second, but Celebrini restored the three-goal lead at 7:40 of the third. He fed Smith cross-ice on the right wing, skated into the slot, and buried the return pass with a wrist shot past Saros' glove for his ninth goal of the season. Steven Stamkos answered on the power play at 16:35 for his 12th of the season, and that was as close as Nashville got.

San Jose was outshot 34-24 but Alex Nedeljkovic stopped 32 of them. The win was the Sharks' second straight night — they were on the second half of a back-to-back after Saturday's 6-5 shootout win at Utah — and their fourth straight game with a point (3-0-1). Nashville, meanwhile, hadn't lost since October 29.

The kids line

The story of the night was the top line's birth certificates: Stenberg is 19, Celebrini is 20, Smith is 21 — and each of them scored. Smith's first-period goal was also his 50th career goal, making him the fifth-fastest Shark ever to reach 50, trailing only Celebrini, Logan Couture, Devin Setoguchi and Pat Falloon.

Celebrini's read on why it clicked was simple: "We were just playing free. We weren't second-guessing ourselves. We were playing fast. That's our game."

He's been playing that way for a while now — 11 points (four goals, seven assists) in his past seven games, and 9 goals with 18 assists through 21 games this season.

The history

The milestone puts Celebrini on a list with exactly the names you'd expect:

1: Wayne Gretzky; 73

2: Mario Lemieux; 31

3: Sidney Crosby; 30

4: Jimmy Carson; 29

5: Macklin Celebrini; 26

5: Dale Hawerchuk; 26

Gretzky's 73 is in another universe — nobody is catching that. The realistic chase is Carson at 29, three ahead, and Celebrini is still 20 with the rest of the season to add to his total before he ages out of the list. For scale on what he's already done: his 115 points last season rank as the sixth-most ever in a single season by a player under 21.

TSL Bets · Nov 16, 2026 · 8:02 AM ET

TSL Bets parlay: Brissett OVER 215.5 passing yards + Nuggets -10.5 (~+270)

By Matt

A Monday night debut against a winless team, and a seven-game winning streak against a winless-on-the-road team. Jacoby Brissett OVER 215.5 passing yards (-111) plus the Nuggets -10.5 (-105) pays about +270.

The game slip — Chargers at Ravens

Los Angeles Chargers (0-8) at Baltimore Ravens (4-5)

Monday, 8:15 PM ET — M&T Bank Stadium, Baltimore — ESPN

• Spread: Ravens -4.5 (-108)

• Total: 39

• Moneyline: Chargers +180 / Ravens -217

• Odds source: BetMGM via SportsBettingDime, November 15; total via Win Daily

Injury report — Chargers: WR Ladd McConkey OUT (back — did not practice all week, will not travel). WR Brenen Thompson OUT (hamstring). S Kendall Williamson OUT (calf), CB Isas Waxter OUT (ankle), OT Branson Taylor OUT (ankle). G Kayode Awosika, DT Justin Eboigbe, OT Trey Pipkins, LB Denzel Perryman and TE David Njoku all questionable.

Injury report — Ravens: QB Lamar Jackson on injured reserve (thumb surgery, 6-to-10-week recovery). QB Tyler Huntley on injured reserve (toe, season-ending). S Jaylinn Hawkins OUT (knee). RB Adam Randall questionable (neck/hamstring). QB Jacoby Brissett — acquired from Arizona on November 6 — will make his Baltimore debut. WR Rashod Bateman, S Kyle Hamilton and CB Nate Wiggins finished the week without designations.

Storylines: The Chargers are 0-8 and haven't won since December 21, 2025. The Ravens are 4-5 after four straight losses, one game outside the AFC's final playoff spot, and have had 11 days since their last game. Brissett is the fourth quarterback to start for Baltimore this season.

The game slip — Suns at Nuggets

Phoenix Suns (5-7) at Denver Nuggets (7-4)

Monday, 9:00 PM ET — Ball Arena, Denver — Altitude / Arizona's Family Sports

• Spread: Nuggets -10.5 (-105)

• Total: 227.5

• Moneyline: Suns +341 / Nuggets -438

• Odds source: Spread — consensus of 11 books via MoneyMaverick, surveyed Monday 6:01 AM ET; moneyline and total — DraftKings via ESPN (BetLegend)

Injury report — Suns: C Mark Williams OUT (shoulder surgery). F Drew Timme day-to-day (hand).

Injury report — Nuggets: G Julian Strawther OUT (knee, six weeks). F Christian Braun (ankle) and F Cam Johnson (biceps) both missed Saturday's win in Minnesota. Devin Booker leads Phoenix at 25.5 points per game.

Storylines: Denver has won seven straight, including Saturday's 123-112 win in Minnesota behind Nikola Jokic's triple-double (27 points, 12 rebounds, 11 assists). Phoenix is 0-3 on the road, losing those games by an average of 18.3 points, and arrives on zero rest after a 131-123 loss to the Lakers on Sunday. Denver won the first meeting 127-120 in Phoenix on October 30.

The bet

Jacoby Brissett OVER 215.5 passing yards (-111, DraftKings via HelloRookie) + Denver Nuggets -10.5 (-105, consensus) — pays about +270

Why these legs

Brissett is a veteran quarterback making his debut for a new team with 11 days to prepare, against the worst team in football. He's thrown for 1,719 yards this season at 214.9 per game — right at the line, which is the honest truth about this leg: the edge isn't the trend, it's the spot. A rested Baltimore offense against an 0-8 Chargers team that is missing starters on both sides of the ball, with a quarterback who has started 46 games in this league and knows how to run an offense cold. The Chargers' defense has given up points to everyone; Brissett doesn't need a career night, he needs his average.

The Nuggets leg is the mismatch. Denver has won seven straight and is at home against a Suns team that is winless on the road, has lost those road games by more than 18 points a night on average, is playing its fourth game in seven days on zero rest, and is missing its starting center. The Nuggets just won in Minnesota without two starters. The 10.5 is a big number, but the road form supports it — and it's the only big-spread leg in this parlay, as it should be.

The honest caveats

Brissett's season average sits a hair under the line, so this leg is a coin flip on the numbers alone — if the Ravens lean on Derrick Henry and the run game, Brissett might not get the volume. And the Nuggets are also shorthanded without Braun and Cam Johnson, so a flat shooting night could keep this inside the number. Two legs, two teams, two sports — that's the point of the parlay, and both legs have to clear on their own.

The record

TSL Bets is 19-32 in November, 23-53 all-time. Two-leg parlays are 4-4 in November at about +250 — the only parlay format that pays.

TSL Bets · Nov 16, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

TSL Bets: Justin Herbert UNDER 200.5 passing yards vs the Ravens (-114)

By Matt

The Chargers are 0-8, Herbert's top two receivers are out, his finger is hurt, and Baltimore's secondary gives up 6.2 net yards per attempt. The play: Herbert UNDER 200.5 passing yards (-114) on Monday Night Football.

The game slip

Los Angeles Chargers (0-8) at Baltimore Ravens (4-5)

Monday, 8:15 PM ET — M&T Bank Stadium, Baltimore — ESPN

• Spread: Ravens -4.5 (-108)

• Total: 39

• Moneyline: Chargers +180 / Ravens -217

• Odds source: BetMGM via SportsBettingDime, November 15; total via Win Daily

Injury report — Chargers: WR Ladd McConkey OUT (back — did not practice all week, will not travel). WR Brenen Thompson OUT (hamstring). S Kendall Williamson OUT (calf), CB Isas Waxter OUT (ankle), OT Branson Taylor OUT (ankle). G Kayode Awosika, DT Justin Eboigbe, OT Trey Pipkins, LB Denzel Perryman and TE David Njoku all questionable. QB Justin Herbert hurt the middle finger on his non-throwing hand last Sunday; X-rays were negative and he is expected to play.

Injury report — Ravens: QB Lamar Jackson on injured reserve (thumb surgery, 6-to-10-week recovery). QB Tyler Huntley on injured reserve (toe, season-ending). S Jaylinn Hawkins OUT (knee). RB Adam Randall questionable (neck/hamstring). QB Jacoby Brissett — acquired from Arizona on November 6 — will make his Baltimore debut. WR Rashod Bateman, S Kyle Hamilton and CB Nate Wiggins finished the week without designations.

Storylines: The Chargers are the league's worst team at 0-8 and haven't won a game since beating Dallas on December 21, 2025. The Ravens are 4-5 after four straight losses and are one game outside the AFC's final playoff spot. Baltimore has had 11 days since its last game. Brissett is the fourth quarterback to start for the Ravens this season, and the Ravens are 7-15 without Lamar Jackson as their starter since 2019, averaging 17.3 points.

The bet

Justin Herbert UNDER 200.5 passing yards (-114, DraftKings via Action Network)

Bleacher Nation lists the same line at 200.5 (-113). The consensus line across books sits between 200.5 and 206.5 — take the under at any number 200.5 or higher.

Why this prop

Herbert is averaging 198.9 passing yards per game this season — 1,591 yards through eight games, with six touchdowns and 11 interceptions. He's been held under 200 passing yards in four of his last six outings, averaging 184.2 yards through the air in that stretch. The number is right at his average, and everything around him points down.

Start with the receivers. McConkey — his top target — is out entirely with a back injury. Thompson, the deep threat, is out with a hamstring. That's the top of the depth chart gone against a Ravens secondary anchored by Kyle Hamilton and Marlon Humphrey that holds opposing quarterbacks to 6.2 net yards per pass attempt.

Then the protection. Herbert has been sacked and pressured into checkdowns all season behind a line that is now also missing Branson Taylor, and the finger injury on his non-throwing hand — even on the glove hand — is the kind of thing that shows up in ball placement on a cold night in Baltimore.

The Chargers as a team average 201.9 passing yards per game, the worst passing offense in the league by the numbers that matter. Nothing about this matchup says 201 or more.

The honest caveats

Game script is the risk. The Ravens are 4.5-point favorites, but if Baltimore jumps ahead early, the Chargers will have to throw — and garbage-time yards count the same as any other. Herbert has cleared 200.5 twice in his last six, so this isn't a lock; it's a number that sits above his average against a defense built to keep him under it. And Brissett's debut could mean short fields for Baltimore, which would mean more possessions — and more throws — for Herbert.

But at 200.5, with his top two receivers out and a top-five secondary waiting, the under is the side.

The record

TSL Bets is 19-32 in November, 23-53 all-time. Props are 5-2 in November — the only winning singles category — that's why the daily single is a prop.

If you or someone you know has a gambling problem, call 1-800-GAMBLER.

Collectibles · Nov 16, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

Raikou ex is the week's biggest mover — up 73% to $132 as Delta Reign heats up

By Matt

Raikou ex from Delta Reign jumped 73% this week to $132 — the biggest gainer in Pokémon — while Yu-Gi-Oh! gained 16% on the month and Pokémon pulled back 8%.

The mover

The week's biggest gainer in Pokémon isn't a vintage chase or a 30th Anniversary grail. It's a brand-new card from the set that just hit shelves.

Raikou ex from Mega Evolution: Delta Reign (ME06) is up 73.0% this week to $132, the largest increase of any Pokémon card in TickerMint's market scanner for the week ending November 15. The move is based on the TCGplayer market price for the card, and it leads a Delta Reign board that is doing most of the hobby's talking right now: Aarune (139/103) is up 39.1% and Kecleon (117/103) is up 37.6% on the same weekly read.

The context matters. Delta Reign is the fresh 2026 print — the buy, at $188.12 for a booster box, against Mega Evolution's $299.85 sell. The set is a week into its life and already producing the week's three biggest gainers, with Raikou ex leading by a wide margin.

The other side of the board

Not everything is rising. The week's biggest drop is Espeon ex 108 — the 30th Celebration promo — down 52.1% to $40. The anniversary set's sealed product is still a second market (standard Elite Trainer Boxes cleared roughly $150-$170 in completed sales after the September 16 launch), but the promo singles are cooling fast.

The broader picture is mixed. Pokémon singles worth $2 or more are up a median 0.6% over 30 days and 6.3% over 90 days — 8,116 cards rose and 5,175 fell in the latest read. The high end of Delta Reign is holding its nerve: Mega Rayquaza ex, the set's chase, has a median of $315 across 21 PriceCharting sales from November 13-14, with a range of $253 to $385, and the special illustration rare has stayed between $296 and $310 for four days.

The month in the hobby

Zoom out a month and the story flips: Yu-Gi-Oh! gained 16% over the past month while Pokémon fell 8%, a 24-percentage-point gap in GempireCards' November market snapshot. Yu-Gi-Oh!'s index is up 47% over three months and 140% over the past year. Magic: The Gathering posted the only other monthly gain among the card categories, rising 1%.

Pokémon still leads on the longer charts — its index sits 84% above its level three months ago and 360% above a year ago — but the month belongs to Yu-Gi-Oh!, and the week belongs to a Raikou that nobody was talking about two weeks ago.

Entertainment · Nov 16, 2026 · 8:00 AM ET

Valkyrae calls out the critics of Vanilla Mace and Katie B's Streamer Awards wins: 'I don't get why some people are so closed-minded'

By Matt

During a livestream on Sunday, Valkyrae pushed back on people discrediting Vanilla Mace and Katie B after their wins at the 2026 Streamer Awards — the latest fallout from Thursday's ceremony in Los Angeles.

The moment

The Streamer Awards were Thursday. The arguments about them are still going.

During a livestream on Sunday, November 15, YouTube and Twitch star Rachell "Valkyrae" Hofstetter called out people criticizing Vanilla Mace and Katie B for winning at the 2026 Streamer Awards, saying she doesn't understand why some people are so closed-minded about the two creators being honored.

Valkyrae — the co-owner of 100 Thieves — was discussing her experience at the annual awards ceremony, hosted by QTCinderella and Caroline Kwan in Los Angeles, when she turned to the criticism the two winners had drawn since Thursday night. Her point was simple: the wins were earned, the discrediting was unwarranted, and she was delighted both creators were honored.

The weekend around it

The sixth annual Streamer Awards, held November 12 in Los Angeles with 37 publicly voted categories, has generated a weekend of fallout. IShowSpeed won Streamer of the Year for the third consecutive year — the first creator ever to win the title three straight times — delivering his entire acceptance speech in Bahasa Indonesia, a nod to his connection with the country since his 2024 visit. He beat fellow nominees Jynxzi, Stable Ronaldo, Joe Bartolozzi and CaseOh.

Elsewhere, Indian creator Ashswag won Best International Streamer over Nigerian streamers Peller and Carter Efe, a result that drew its own debate — former Big Brother Naija winner Phyna alleged the organizers used Nigerian nominees to promote the event, a claim that is her allegation and not a finding, with no voting breakdown published.

Valkyrae's defense of Vanilla Mace and Katie B lands in that same churn: a weekend where every result got re-litigated in clips and streams. The difference is that Valkyrae wasn't arguing about a result. She was arguing about the impulse to tear one down.

Gaming · Nov 16, 2026 · 8:00 AM ET

BLG beats Gen.G 3-1 to win Worlds 2026 — China's first Summoner's Cup in five years

By Matt

Bilibili Gaming dropped the opening game, then won three straight to beat Gen.G 3-1 in the Worlds 2026 final at Barclays Center — the organization's first title, the LPL's first since 2021, and the end of Korea's four-year run.

The final

Gen.G won the opening game emphatically — 19 kills to 4 in 39 minutes — and for a moment it looked like another Korean coronation. Then Bilibili Gaming did what it had done to every Korean team in its path: it adjusted, it absorbed, and it won three straight.

BLG beat Gen.G 3-1 in the League of Legends World Championship final at Barclays Center in Brooklyn, claiming the organization's first world title in only its third Worlds appearance. Game 2 stretched to 53 minutes — the longest game of the tournament — before BLG leveled the series. Game 3 ended in 37 minutes. Game 4 closed it in 42, with top laner Chen "Bin" Ze-Bin on Gwen and BLG seizing Baron before destroying Gen.G's Nexus.

The result ends a five-year drought for China's LPL — the first Chinese team to lift the Summoner's Cup since EDward Gaming in 2021 — and a four-year run of Korean Worlds titles. BLG took home $1 million of the $5 million prize pool; Gen.G received $800,000 as runners-up.

One piercing four

The final was the culmination of a knockout run the Chinese press dubbed "one piercing four" — BLG personally eliminated all four Korean seeds on its way to the trophy. It beat Hanwha Life Esports in the qualifying stage, swept Dplus KIA 3-0 in the quarterfinals, edged defending champion T1 3-1 in the semifinal, and then overcame Gen.G in the final.

Mid laner Zhuo "Knight" Ding was named Finals MVP, winning his first world title after years as one of the best players never to lift the Cup. Bin, Xun and ON claimed their first titles alongside him. And marksman Park "Viper" Do-hyeon won his second — five years after his first with EDward Gaming in 2021 — becoming a two-time world champion as an LPL player.

What came next

Hours after the final, Riot Games dropped a teaser for League of Legends: New Dawn, with a full reveal planned for January 2027 and a release later in 2027. The timing was no accident: the biggest stage in the sport, a new champion, and a first look at what's next for the game itself.

One footnote on the night: the final peaked at 3.98 million viewers, the lowest since 2020, per Esports Charts — a number that leaves out Chinese streaming platforms entirely, and one shaped by a 3 AM start in China, a 4 AM start in Korea, and a final without T1. The audience that counts most for this result — the one in China, watching a Chinese team end a five-year wait at 3 in the morning — isn't in that number at all.

• China's BLG Beats Gen.G for First Worlds Title, Ending Korea's Four-Year Reign (Seoul Economic Daily)

• Worlds 2026 Final Peaked at 3.98M Viewers, Lowest Since 2020 (TalkEsport)

Sports · Nov 16, 2026 · 8:00 AM ET

Kirk Cousins is back — and the Vikings are back with him in a 30-10 rout of the Packers at Lambeau

By Matt

Five days after being reacquired from Las Vegas, Kirk Cousins threw two touchdowns in his first Vikings start since 2023 — on the same field where he tore his Achilles — as Minnesota routed Green Bay 30-10.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Kirk Cousins' last start for the Minnesota Vikings ended with a torn Achilles at Lambeau Field in Week 8 of 2023. His first start back in purple came on the same field on Sunday — and this time, he walked off it a winner.

Cousins completed 21 of 31 passes for 207 yards and two touchdowns as the Vikings routed the Green Bay Packers 30-10 at Lambeau Field, five days after Minnesota reacquired him from the Las Vegas Raiders in a trade-deadline deal for a third-round pick. His voice cracked speaking with ESPN's Lindsey Thiry afterward as he reflected on the journey back.

The numbers weren't gaudy — a 94.4 passer rating, one interception — but the performance was exactly what the Vikings needed: calm, efficient, and mistake-free when it mattered. After just two practices with the team, it was called the Vikings' best quarterback performance of the season.

The moments

Cousins' first touchdown was the throw that announced he was home. On third-and-23 late in the first quarter, he dropped a perfectly placed pass to Myles Price for a 38-yard score and a 10-3 lead. His second came when the Packers were threatening to turn the game around: after Green Bay outgained Minnesota 158 to 26 in the third quarter to halve the lead, Cousins went 8-for-8 for 68 yards on the answering drive, capped by a 20-yard touchdown pass to Gavin Bartholomew for a 17-point lead with 8:29 remaining.

The defense did the rest. Minnesota forced four turnovers and sacked Jordan Love eight times — Levi Drake Rodriguez had two of them — and the Vikings held the ball for 35 minutes and 16 seconds. Green Bay's only touchdown came on a 32-yard pass to J. Michael Sturdivant in the third quarter, after Minnesota had already scored 49 straight points against the Packers across the two meetings this season.

The win was the Vikings' most points since their 39-22 shellacking of these same Packers in Week 1, and it completed the season sweep of the Border Battle — Minnesota's fifth win in six meetings with Green Bay. Head coach Kevin O'Connell recorded his 50th career victory.

What it means

The Vikings snapped a two-game losing streak, improved to 7-2, and joined the Detroit Lions and Chicago Bears in a three-way tie atop the NFC North. The quarterback situation that had defined their season — Kyler Murray out, Carson Wentz ineffective last week — suddenly looks solved, at least for now: the 38-year-old who started 88 games for the franchise from 2018 to 2023 is back in the No. 8 jersey, and the team that needed him most finally has him.

Next up is a four-game gauntlet that closes with the San Francisco 49ers on a neutral field in Mexico City on Sunday night. If Cousins plays like he did at Lambeau, the Vikings will like their chances in all four.

Sports · Nov 16, 2026 · 8:00 AM ET

James Cook runs into history — 157 yards, a 74-yard dagger, and a streak only four players have ever touched

By Matt

James Cook rushed 18 times for 157 yards and a touchdown against the Jets, becoming just the fifth player in NFL history with at least 75 rushing yards and a rushing touchdown in eight consecutive games.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Jets lost a fumble on a failed hook-and-lateral in the fourth quarter. Ten seconds later, James Cook made them pay for it — and made history in the process.

Cook took a handoff, broke free down the right side, and ripped off a 74-yard touchdown run that put Buffalo's 31-12 win over the Jets out of reach with 4:42 remaining. He finished with 18 carries for 157 yards and the score, and the run itself was a small piece of physics: 21.56 mph on the play, according to Next Gen Stats, the second-fastest touchdown run of his NFL career. It was Buffalo's longest run of the season and the second-longest in the NFL this year.

But the 74-yarder was only the exclamation point. The sentence it finished is the story: Cook became just the fifth player in NFL history with at least 75 rushing yards and a rushing touchdown in eight consecutive games.

The company

Here are the longest such streaks in NFL history:

• Terry Allen, Washington: 10 games (1995-96)

• John Riggins, Washington: 8 games (1983)

• George Rogers, Washington: 8 games (1985-86)

• LaDainian Tomlinson, San Diego Chargers: 8 games (2006)

• James Cook, Buffalo Bills: 8 games (2026)

Cook is the first to do it since Tomlinson in 2006 — the year the Hall of Fame running back was named NFL MVP. Three of the other four names on the list are in the Hall of Fame or on the short list of the greatest running backs ever to play. Cook now shares a line with them.

He also has at least 100 yards from scrimmage and a touchdown in five consecutive games, and a rushing touchdown in eight straight — a streak that has quietly become the engine of the best team in the AFC.

The team around him

Buffalo improved to 8-1 with its fifth consecutive win, and the Bills did it the way they've done it all season: a defense that gave up yards (376) but no touchdowns, four sacks, 12 quarterback hits and two takeaways, and an offense that needed just seven plays to score three second-half touchdowns covering 202 yards in 2 minutes, 49 seconds. Cook's 74-yarder was the last of them — one play, 74 yards, ten seconds.

The Bills are 8-1 for the first time since 1991 and have won eight straight road games. Cook has now rushed for 814 yards on the season, and the Jets — who came in having lost to Buffalo five straight times — fell to 2-8, with head coach Aaron Glenn now 0-9 against AFC East opponents.

Cook's own reaction to the speed number was the same as his coach's: both Josh Allen and head coach Joe Brady wondered aloud after the game how fast he was running. The answer — 21.56 mph — is now in the books next to a streak that only four players in the history of the league have ever touched.

Sports · Nov 16, 2026 · 7:59 AM ET

Josh Allen passes Peyton Manning — 325 total touchdowns, the most ever through a player's first 10 seasons

By Matt

Josh Allen entered Sunday tied with Peyton Manning at 322 career total touchdowns. A one-yard run in the first quarter put him past the Hall of Famer — and he finished the day at 325, with a season and a half still to add to the record.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Josh Allen needed one touchdown. He got it early, and then he kept going.

Allen entered Sunday's game against the New York Jets tied with Hall of Fame quarterback Peyton Manning at 322 career total touchdowns — the most by any player through his first 10 NFL seasons. On Buffalo's second possession, after a defensive holding call gave the Bills first-and-goal at the one-foot line, Allen called his own number and punched in a one-yard rushing score. Career touchdown No. 323. Manning, passed.

Allen wasn't done. He added another one-yard rushing touchdown in the third quarter and a 45-yard touchdown pass to DJ Moore, finishing the day with three total touchdowns and a career total of 325 — 233 passing, 90 rushing and two receiving — in a 31-12 Bills win at MetLife Stadium that improved Buffalo to 8-1.

The gap in the numbers

The record is impressive on its own. The way Allen got there is the jaw-dropper.

Manning needed 160 regular-season games to pile up his 322 touchdowns across his first 10 seasons in Indianapolis. Allen passed that total in just 137 games — and he did it before completing his ninth season. He still has the rest of this season and all of 2027 to add to a record that now belongs to him alone.

The one-yard run that broke the record was also his 10th rushing touchdown of the 2026 season, extending his streak to four consecutive years with at least 10 rushing scores — tying Jalen Hurts (2021-24) for the longest such streak by a quarterback in NFL history. His second rushing touchdown of the day was the 90th of his career, tying Eric Dickerson and Curtis Martin for 14th in NFL history; his next one ties Jerome Bettis and Franco Harris for 12th.

Allen's own assessment was characteristically blunt: he was critical of his own performance, an 11-of-21 passing day for 196 yards and an interception. The record didn't care. Neither did the scoreboard.

The team around him

Buffalo's win was its fifth straight, and the Bills are 8-1 for the first time since 1991 — the year they finished 13-3 and reached the second of four consecutive Super Bowls. They've won eight consecutive road games dating to last season and are the NFL's only unbeaten team on the road in 2026.

The defense did its part: four sacks, 12 quarterback hits, two takeaways, and zero touchdowns allowed over the last eight quarters — the Jets managed four field goals and went 0-for-2 in the red zone. Geno Smith left the game briefly but cleared concussion protocol and returned, finishing 13-of-21 for 148 yards. Garrett Wilson caught seven passes for 106 yards in the loss.

Hall of Fame quarterback Jim Kelly posted a short message for Allen on Instagram after the game. He didn't need many words. Nobody does, when the number is 325 and counting.

Sports · Nov 16, 2026 · 7:59 AM ET

Ja'Marr Chase goes for 231 as Joe Burrow throws for 409 — the Bengals own Sunday night and the AFC North

By Matt

Joe Burrow threw for 409 yards and four touchdowns and Ja'Marr Chase caught 11 passes for 231 yards as Cincinnati dominated Pittsburgh 38-18 on Sunday Night Football, moving into sole possession of first place in the AFC North.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Bengals didn't just beat the Steelers on Sunday night. They ambushed them — and the two players who did the ambushing put up numbers that will be talked about all week.

Joe Burrow passed for 409 yards and four touchdowns, Ja'Marr Chase caught 11 passes for 231 yards, and Cincinnati dominated Pittsburgh 38-18 at Paycor Stadium to move into sole possession of first place in the AFC North. The Bengals jumped out early and never let the Steelers back in, outgaining Pittsburgh 484-222 in a game that was effectively over long before the fourth quarter.

Burrow completed 35 of 44 passes, and his 409 yards were the most the Steelers' defense has allowed in four seasons. Chase was the main beneficiary — 11 catches, 231 yards, a night where every time Burrow needed a play, his No. 1 receiver was open. The Bengals scored 21 points in the second quarter alone and took a lead they never surrendered.

The other side of the rout

The Steelers came in riding a four-game winning streak after their bye, and their running game had powered it. Sunday night, it never got going. Jaylen Warren — who ran for 127 yards against these same Bengals in Week 3 — was held to seven carries for 15 yards, finishing behind Rico Dowdle and Lew Nichols in Pittsburgh's backfield. Aaron Rodgers finished 15-of-29 for 143 yards and two touchdowns, and the Steelers managed just 23 yards of total offense through three quarters, actually losing 17 yards in the third.

Pittsburgh's offense didn't score until the game was out of reach. The Bengals' defense, which had been the story of this rivalry in the other direction all season, held the Steelers to 0-for-2 in the red zone and made the league's sixth-leading rusher in scrimmage yards a non-factor.

What it means

The win splits the season series for the third straight season, but the standings tell the bigger story: Cincinnati alone atop the AFC North, with the team that beat them in Week 3 now chasing. The Bengals' 38 points came from a quarterback playing the best football of his season and a receiver who looks uncoverable right now — and if Burrow and Chase keep doing this, the North runs through Cincinnati.

Pittsburgh, at 6-3, isn't out of anything. But Sunday night was a reminder of how quickly a four-game winning streak can evaporate when the quarterback throws for 409 and your run game gets 15 yards from its lead back.

NHL · Nov 16, 2026 · 7:59 AM ET

Anton Forsberg blanks his old team — 17 saves, 1-0 — as the Kings win their fourth straight in Ottawa

By Matt

Anton Forsberg stopped all 17 shots he faced in his return to Ottawa — his first shutout of the season and ninth of his career — as Los Angeles blanked the Senators 1-0 for its fourth straight win.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Anton Forsberg spent parts of five seasons in Ottawa. On Saturday night, he went back to Canadian Tire Centre wearing a different jersey and made sure his old team remembered exactly what he can do.

Forsberg made 17 saves for the Los Angeles Kings in a 1-0 win over the Senators — his first shutout of the season, the ninth of his NHL career, and a win against the franchise he played for from 2020 to 2025. He improved his career record to 3-0-0 against his former club, and he preserved the shutout in style, stopping Ridly Greig on a breakaway with 11:21 left in the third.

Alex Laferriere scored the game's only goal, tipping a Mikey Anderson point shot at 9:19 of the first period. Linus Ullmark made 17 saves at the other end for Ottawa. That was the entire scoring line. One goal, 34 combined saves, and a goaltender who spent five seasons learning that building deciding he wasn't going to let anything in.

The road Kings

The win was Los Angeles' fourth in a row and its ninth road win of the season — the most in the NHL — and the sixth straight win on the road this fall. The Kings improved to 10-5-4 and moved into first place in the Pacific Division for the first time this season, opening a six-game Eastern road trip with four straight wins.

Ottawa, meanwhile, saw its seven-game point streak (4-0-3) come to an end, falling to 9-6-4. Laferriere extended his point streak to four games, and the Kings' second shutout win in as many weeks against the Senators in Ottawa underscored how comfortable this team is playing away from home.

The win came at a cost: top defenseman Drew Doughty left the game in the second period after blocking a shot, a loss the Kings will be watching closely as the trip continues to Washington.

NHL · Nov 16, 2026 · 7:58 AM ET

Jesper Wallstedt's second straight shutout — 28 saves, zero answers — lifts the Wild past the Ducks

By Matt

Jesper Wallstedt stopped all 28 shots he faced for his second consecutive shutout — and the third of his career — as Minnesota blanked Anaheim 2-0, making him the first Wild rookie ever to post back-to-back shutouts.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Minnesota Wild are starting to look like a team that's turning a corner, and the clearest sign is the 23-year-old in their net who won't let anything past him.

Jesper Wallstedt made 28 saves for his second straight shutout on Saturday night as the Wild beat the Anaheim Ducks 2-0 at Grand Casino Arena in St. Paul. It was his second shutout of the season and the third of his young career — and it made him the first Wild rookie ever to post back-to-back shutouts.

Wallstedt blanked Calgary 2-0 on 36 saves in his previous start, then did it again six days later against a Ducks team that came in as the second-highest scoring team in the NHL at 3.88 goals per game. His shutout streak reached 141:09 and counting. His biggest save of the night came late in the second period, when he robbed Frank Vatrano alone in front to keep the game scoreless.

Marcus Johansson scored the goal Minnesota needed 55 seconds into the second period, Matt Boldy added a goal and an assist — including the empty-netter with 55 seconds left — and Petr Mrazek stopped 29 shots at the other end for Anaheim. It didn't matter. Nobody was beating Wallstedt on this night.

The Wall of St. Paul

Wallstedt turned 23 on Friday. He is a first-round pick from 2021, the backup to Filip Gustavsson — the fellow Swede who signed a five-year, $34 million extension on October 4 — and he is playing like a goaltender who intends to make the tandem question uncomfortable. He improved to 4-0-2 as a starter, has two shutouts in six starts this season, and three in 11 starts across his three-year NHL career.

The Wild needed him. Minnesota climbed back above .500 for the first time in a month at 8-7-4, has gone 5-1-1 in its last seven games while allowing just 12 goals in that stretch, and is now 19-1-0 against the Ducks dating to the 2020-21 season, including six straight wins. The power play went 0-for-7 and the offense managed just enough — but when your goalie hasn't allowed a goal in 141 minutes, just enough is plenty.

Anaheim, for its part, is still dangerous — just not on this night, and not against this goalie. The Ducks will get their rematch in Minnesota to close the regular season on April 14. Wallstedt will be waiting.

NHL · Nov 16, 2026 · 7:58 AM ET

Jason Robertson's natural hat trick — his sixth career — powers the Stars past the Flyers for a fifth straight win

By Matt

Jason Robertson scored a natural hat trick — two in the second period, one on the third-period power play — as Dallas beat Philadelphia 5-1 for its fifth straight win, his third straight three-point game.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Jason Robertson didn't just score three goals on Saturday night. He scored the only three that mattered, in order, without interruption — a natural hat trick that turned a tight game into a rout and kept the Dallas Stars rolling.

Robertson struck twice in the second period and once more on a third-period power play as the Stars beat the Philadelphia Flyers 5-1 at American Airlines Center for their fifth consecutive win. It was his sixth career hat trick, his second career natural hat trick, and his third straight three-point game.

Lian Bichsel opened the scoring with his first goal of the season, and then Robertson took over. He doubled the lead at 11:39 of the middle period, tapping in a loose puck after Tyler Seguin lost control of it on a partial break, then scored again before the period ended. He completed the natural hat trick at 9:29 of the third, snapping a shot from the slot under the glove of Flyers goaltender Dan Vladar on the power play. Roope Hintz assisted on all three goals.

Seguin added a goal and an assist of his own, Christian Dvorak scored Philadelphia's only goal, and the Stars closed out a 5-1 win that was never really in doubt after Robertson's second.

The hottest week in hockey

Robertson didn't just have a big night — he had the biggest week in the NHL. He was named the league's First Star of the Week for his six goals and nine points across three games, a stretch that started with a comeback win over Ottawa on November 11 and peaked with Saturday's hat trick.

The numbers stack up fast: a four-game point streak (six goals, four assists), 14 points over his last eight games, and three straight three-point games. When the Stars host the Islanders on Wednesday, Robertson has a chance to become the first player in Dallas franchise history to record four consecutive three-point games.

Dallas improved to 12-4-3 with the win and has points in 12 of its last 13 games (9-1-3), tied for the second-best record in the NHL. The Stars have won 10 of their last 11 meetings with Philadelphia and nine straight at home against the Flyers — and they've outscored their opponents 12-1 over the last two games.

The Flyers fell to 9-6-3, their five-game point streak over. Before the puck dropped, Dallas announced defenseman Thomas Harley is out week-to-week with a lower-body injury — a notable absence for a team that keeps winning anyway.

Sports · Nov 16, 2026 · 7:57 AM ET

Jokic's triple-double — 27, 12 and 11 — carries the Nuggets to a seventh straight win in Minnesota

By Matt

Nikola Jokic posted 27 points, 12 rebounds and 11 assists — his seventh triple-double of the season — as Denver erased a halftime deficit and beat Minnesota 123-112 for its seventh straight win, with three teammates scoring 23 apiece.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The stat sheet broke before Nikola Jokic did.

A glitch in the NBA's courtside statistics system froze the stat-keeping program late in the third quarter at Target Center on Saturday night, and the final numbers weren't available until hours after the game ended. When they finally landed, they said what everyone in the building already knew: Jokic had 27 points, 12 rebounds and 11 assists, and the Denver Nuggets had their seventh straight win, 123-112 over the Minnesota Timberwolves.

It was Jokic's seventh triple-double of the season, and it came on a night when Denver needed all of it. Minnesota led 60-55 at halftime and had Julius Randle and Anthony Edwards going for 26 points apiece. The Nuggets answered by outscoring the Timberwolves 68-52 in the second half, and Jokic — playing on a sore wrist that had him listed as questionable and a game-time decision — was the one bending the game every time Minnesota threatened to take it back.

He shot 8-of-15 from the field, 3-of-6 from three and a perfect 8-of-8 from the line in 42 minutes. The 11 assists were the corrected final figure after the stat-system crash; early reports had him at 10, and the league's official box score settled it at 11.

Everybody eats

What made the night unusual wasn't just Jokic. It was how little he had to do alone.

Tim Hardaway Jr., Aaron Gordon and Jamal Murray scored 23 points apiece — three teammates matching each other exactly — while Gordon added 10 rebounds and Murray dished out 12 assists. Denver's second half was a clinic in shared offense: four Nuggets finished with at least 23 points, and the ball kept finding the open man no matter who Minnesota tried to take away.

That matters because Denver is doing this shorthanded. The Nuggets were missing both starting forwards: Christian Braun, out with a left ankle sprain, and Cam Johnson, out with a right biceps strain, both hurt during Wednesday's win over the Clippers. Jokic played through his own wrist issue, and the depth behind him delivered the best win of the streak.

Minnesota, meanwhile, saw its four-game winning streak snapped. Randle and Edwards each scored 26, but the Timberwolves got outscored by 16 after halftime and never found a second-half answer for a Denver team that kept getting whatever shot it wanted.

The chase, quietly

Jokic's triple-double counting is becoming a background drumbeat: seven this season, and now sitting within single digits of Russell Westbrook's all-time record of 209. Nobody in Denver is talking about it as a chase — the Nuggets are talking about a seven-game winning streak and a team that keeps finding new ways to win while banged up. But the number is there, and at this rate it stops being a question of if and becomes a question of when.

The Wolves get another crack at Denver soon enough — these Northwest Division rivals have now met twice in Minneapolis this season, with the Nuggets taking both, and twice in the playoffs in the last three years. Saturday night was the latest chapter, and it belonged to the big man whose stat line needed hours to be believed.

Collectibles · Nov 15, 2026 · 9:09 PM ET

Mega Evolution is the sell, Delta Reign the buy

By Matt

PokeInsider called the Mega Evolution booster box a sell at $299.85 on TCGplayer — and the buy is the fresh 2026 print, Delta Reign, at $188.12.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The call

PokeInsider published its sealed-box call on November 15: sell the Mega Evolution booster box, sell Phantasmal Flames, and buy Delta Reign instead. The logic is the print date. The two boxes on the sell side were printed before the early 2026 market jump, so their prices are anchored to a market that no longer exists. The buy side is a box printed in 2026, released nine days ago, and already sliding toward its list price.

Mega Evolution: $299.85; $290.00

Phantasmal Flames: $380.34; —

Mega Evolution: Delta Reign: $188.12; $181.00

Why the older boxes are sells

The Mega Evolution box is sitting, not moving. PokeInsider's numbers show 31 listings and just five sales in the week of November 9 — thin demand at a price set before the market reset. The call is a sell at $290.00 or better, and a sell on Phantasmal Flames near its current level too. Both boxes are priced off the pre-jump market, and the market has moved on.

Why Delta Reign is the buy

Delta Reign released on November 6, 2026, and it is trading like a live box, not a museum piece: 101 listings, with 795 sales across November 13–15 alone. The price has been coming down steadily since release day — $212.18 on November 6, $208.42 by November 8, and lower still today — while the older boxes sit flat.

That trajectory is the point. A 2026 print with real sales volume and a falling price is a box you can actually buy, open, or hold without paying a premium for a print run from before the jump.

The 2026 class

Delta Reign is not the only 2026-printed box trading near list. Perfect Order, Pitch Black and Chaos Rising sit at $168.64, $176.21 and $180.01 respectively, with Delta Reign the priciest of the four — and all four cluster within about twenty dollars of each other. The gap between the 2026 prints and the pre-jump boxes is the story: the newer the print, the closer the price is to what the box actually costs to make and move.

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 9:04 PM ET

46-40! The 49ers and Cowboys just played the first game in NFL history to end that way

By Matt

San Francisco outlasted Dallas 46-40 at AT&T Stadium in the first 46-40 final in NFL history — a 36-point fourth quarter, 584 yards of 49ers offense, and four stars rewriting record books on the same night.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The first 46-40 ever

The San Francisco 49ers beat the Dallas Cowboys 46-40 on Sunday at AT&T Stadium, and the final score had never happened before in NFL history. No game had ever ended 46-40 — a genuine Scorigami, on a day when the offenses made it look easy.

The fourth quarter alone produced 36 points. San Francisco piled up 584 total yards, the fifth-most in 49ers history and the third-most Dallas has ever allowed — and the most the Cowboys have ever given up in a home game. The win moved San Francisco to 8-1, still atop the NFC West. Dallas fell to 4-6.

This was the 42nd meeting between the two franchises, and San Francisco's fifth straight win in the rivalry. The 49ers now lead the all-time series 22-19-1. It was also, per the Associated Press, the first time in Cowboys history that a defense allowed both a 70-plus-yard touchdown run and a 70-plus-yard touchdown pass in the same game. And per the Elias Sports Bureau, San Francisco's three touchdowns of 60-plus yards tied the most by any team in a game over the last 50 years.

49ers: 7; 10; 14; 15; 46

Cowboys: 10; 6; 3; 21; 40

Dallas actually led for much of the first half, going up 16-10 in the second quarter before San Francisco answered with a 28-3 run that built a 38-19 fourth-quarter lead. The Cowboys then scored 21 in the final frame, twice cutting the deficit to six, but never got the ball back with a chance to tie. The last Dallas drive ended at its own 9-yard line with nine seconds left, after a deep shot fell incomplete and a series of laterals went nowhere.

Brock Purdy: a career day, under pressure all night

Purdy finished 22-of-28 for 389 yards with three touchdowns and no interceptions, a 154.5 passer rating, and added 21 yards on two carries. The yardage total is the best of his career.

It did not come clean. Dallas sacked him five times, four of them in the first half, and still he completed 78.6 percent of his throws. The signature play came with 2:26 left in the second quarter: on third-and-8 from his own 26, Purdy escaped the blitz, bought time, and found Christian McCaffrey releasing out of the backfield. McCaffrey caught it at the 40 and took it 60 more yards for the score that gave San Francisco a 17-16 halftime lead.

Purdy is now 4-0 all-time against Dallas. He entered the game with five touchdowns and no interceptions in the rivalry, and added three more scores to that ledger on Sunday.

Christian McCaffrey: the most dangerous catch in football

McCaffrey caught all four of his targets for 161 yards and two touchdowns, and ran 13 times for 62 yards and a score. That is 223 scrimmage yards and three total touchdowns, with the receiving yardage a career high.

Both receiving touchdowns were career-long plays: a 74-yarder just before halftime and a 66-yarder late in the fourth, when Dallas inexplicably had linebacker Von Miller in coverage on him. His rushing score was a 16-yard burst off left tackle that stretched across the goal line and made it 38-19.

The 74-yarder was also his 38th career receiving touchdown, extending the running-back record he set a week ago in Las Vegas by passing Marshall Faulk. San Francisco's ground game as a whole ran for 214 yards at 7.4 yards a carry, and rookie Kaelon Black added a 25-yard touchdown in the third quarter, running clean through two defensive backs. Keenan Allen, in his 49ers debut, caught five passes for 61 yards.

Dak Prescott: five touchdowns, two records, and still a loss

Prescott went 30-of-51 for 343 yards with five touchdowns and one interception, a 103.6 rating. The five touchdown passes tie his career high, the second time he has done it.

The fifth of them made history. His fourth-quarter touchdown to CeeDee Lamb was the 249th passing touchdown of his career, passing Tony Romo's 248 for the Cowboys' franchise record. Prescott set a franchise mark on a night his team lost by six — the cruelest kind of milestone game.

Lamb's touchdown, a 1-yarder with 47 seconds left, plus Prescott's two-point conversion run, cut the lead to 46-40. Ryan Flournoy had caught an 11-yard score earlier in the rally and finished with 71 yards. But the onside kick was recovered by San Francisco, and Dallas never saw the ball again with a real chance.

George Pickens: the return, the milestone, the hat trick

Pickens caught 11 passes for 145 yards and three touchdowns on 18 targets, with a long of 39. All three marks tell the same story: a career high in touchdown catches, a season high in yards, in his first game back from the hamstring injury that cost him Week 9.

The first score did the historical work. His 35-yard first-quarter touchdown catch pushed him past 2,000 receiving yards as a Cowboy — and made him the third Cowboy ever to reach that mark in his first 26 games with the franchise, joining Terrell Owens (2,208) and Bob Hayes (2,136). His other two scores went for 39 and 5 yards. Lamb finished with seven catches for 72 yards and the record-setting touchdown.

What it means

San Francisco stays on the road this week, practicing in Colorado Springs before a Sunday night showdown with the 7-2 Minnesota Vikings in Mexico City on November 22. Dallas, now 4-6, hosts the Tennessee Titans next Sunday. The 49ers look like the NFC's team to beat; the Cowboys look like a team that can score with anyone and still lose, because on Sunday even a franchise-record night from their quarterback and a hat trick from their No. 1 receiver were not enough.

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:59 PM ET

Patrick Mahomes throws five touchdowns, no picks, as the Chiefs roll Atlanta 35-13

By Matt

Patrick Mahomes threw five touchdown passes to four different receivers in a 35-13 win over Atlanta — his first five-touchdown game since 2022 — as rookie Emmett Johnson ran for 101 yards in his first start.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

Five touchdowns, four receivers, zero picks

Patrick Mahomes threw five touchdown passes and no interceptions as the Kansas City Chiefs beat the Atlanta Falcons 35-13 on Sunday at Mercedes-Benz Stadium. The final line: 22-of-35 for 348 yards, a 135.5 rating, plus 15 yards on two carries.

It was his first five-touchdown game since 2022, and the seventh time in his career he has thrown five in a game. The five scores went to four different receivers, and three of them were firsts: a first career touchdown catch for rookie Cyrus Allen, a first career touchdown catch for Andrew Armstrong, and a first touchdown as a Chief for Josh Downs, acquired from Indianapolis on Tuesday.

Kansas City improved to 7-2 and moved into a first-place tie in the AFC West with Denver, which had its bye week. Atlanta fell to 3-7, losers of three straight and five of six.

How the game went

Kansas City scored on its opening drive, a 10-play, 80-yard march capped by a 12-yard touchdown to Emmett Johnson, and never trailed. Downs made it 14-3 with an 18-yard score late in the first half, and the Chiefs led by that margin at the break.

The second half followed the same script. After Atlanta was stopped on fourth down at midfield, Mahomes went deep to Allen for a 39-yard touchdown to make it 21-3. Johnson's second score, a 6-yarder early in the fourth, stretched the lead to 28-3. Atlanta was held without a first down on its first four possessions of the game, and Kansas City piled up 461 total yards.

The Falcons did make it interesting for about four minutes. Bijan Robinson scored on a 3-yard run midway through the fourth, Chris Blair recovered the league's first successful onside kick this season, and a Jake Moody field goal cut it to 28-13. Mahomes answered on the next drive that mattered, hitting Armstrong for a 43-yard touchdown with 4:18 left to close the scoring at 35-13. A late Atlanta drive reached the Kansas City 6 before Kristian Fulton broke up Penix's final pass as time expired. Nohl Williams also picked off Penix in the end zone.

Emmett Johnson: the next man up, all the way up

Johnson made his first start with Kenneth Walker III ruled out on Saturday because of a groin injury, and he looked like a starter. The rookie carried 22 times for 101 yards and caught six passes for 70 yards and two touchdowns — 171 total yards on 28 touches.

Both of his scores came on Mahomes throws in space, the 12-yarder that opened the scoring and the 6-yarder that made it 28-3. Rashee Rice led the Chiefs in receiving with five catches for 82 yards, including a 50-yard gain that set up Johnson's second touchdown.

The context matters here. Kansas City's offense was already depleted — Walker out, Travis Kelce leaving early with an ankle injury and not returning — and Mahomes still moved the ball at will with a rookie running back and receivers catching their first career touchdowns. That is the version of the Chiefs that wins in January.

Atlanta: the slide continues

Penix finished 23-of-43 for 286 yards with an interception. Robinson, the NFL's rushing leader, ran 16 times for 71 yards and the Falcons' only touchdown. Drake London led Atlanta with six catches for 109 yards. Moody, signed off Baltimore's practice squad on Wednesday after Nick Folk was cut, made a 28-yard field goal in the second quarter for Atlanta's only first-half points.

The Falcons have now lost three straight. They get a bye week next, then visit Minnesota on November 29. Kansas City hosts the Arizona Cardinals next Sunday, then a Thanksgiving showdown with the Bills.

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:58 PM ET

54 yards, as time expires: Ryan Fitzgerald walks off the Saints in New Orleans

By Matt

Charlie Smyth tied it from 55 with 41 seconds left. Ryan Fitzgerald answered from 54 as time expired — his fifth career game-winner, and first place in the NFC South for Carolina.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The day

The Carolina Panthers beat the New Orleans Saints 20-17 at Caesars Superdome on Sunday, and the ending was as wild as a division game gets. Ryan Fitzgerald hit a 54-yard field goal as time expired to win it — 41 seconds after Charlie Smyth tied the game with a 55-yarder of his own. Fitzgerald finished 2-for-3 on field goals. Chuba Hubbard carried 27 times for 92 yards and two touchdowns and added five catches for 80 yards, for 172 total yards. Bryce Young went 17-of-30 for 200 yards with no touchdowns and no interceptions. Tyler Shough finished 25-of-35 for 187 yards with one touchdown and one interception.

The final minute

With 1:43 left, Shough drove New Orleans 41 yards in eight plays to set up Smyth, who drilled the longest field goal of his young career to tie it at 17 with 41 seconds on the clock. The Superdome had barely finished roaring when Carolina got the ball back — and a defensive pass interference call on Saints cornerback Kool-Aid McKinstry against Jalen Coker moved the Panthers to the New Orleans 36-yard line with 21 seconds left.

One Hubbard rush later, Fitzgerald walked on with two seconds remaining and knocked it through right down the middle. It was the longest walk-off field goal in Panthers history, and the fifth game-winner of his young career — a franchise record for a kicker in his first two years with the team, and the third-most game-winners by any Panthers kicker overall. He knew it was good the second it left his foot.

Hubbard carried them

None of it happens without Hubbard, who was Carolina's offense all afternoon. His one-yard touchdown run at 1:49 had given the Panthers the lead in the first place, capping a day where he scored twice on the ground and did damage as a receiver, including a 34-yard catch-and-run that put Carolina on the doorstep. The pregame speech was his, the carries were his, and when the game was on the line, the ball kept finding him.

The kicker's day

Fitzgerald's afternoon started badly. He missed a 48-yarder in the second quarter, sailing it just outside the left upright — an overcorrection, by his own admission, after spending the week fixing a run of misses to the right. He gives himself about two minutes to be upset about a miss, then gets back in the net and moves on. The next opportunity took an hour to arrive: a 55-yarder late in the third quarter, attempted from the Saints' 37 on fourth-and-5, that put Carolina up 10-7. He was already halfway onto the field before head coach Dave Canales had finished deciding to try it.

The track record is why the trust was there. Fitzgerald hit four game-winners as a rookie last season, tying the modern NFL rookie record, and his teammates have a standing rule about him in those moments: nobody talks to the kicker. Just let him work.

The stakes and the mess

The winner took sole possession of first place in the NFC South, and both teams came in at 3-5 — which tells you plenty about the division, and plenty about why this one mattered. Carolina is now 4-5 and snapped a three-game losing streak; New Orleans fell to 3-6.

It was not a clean win. Carolina was flagged for a franchise-record 17 accepted penalties for 152 yards, and the Saints' only lead of the day came on Alvin Kamara's three-yard touchdown run in the third quarter. But the Panthers kept answering — Fitzgerald's 55-yarder after Kamara's score, Hubbard's go-ahead touchdown, and then the walk-off. In a division this tight, that is enough.

By the numbers

Panthers record: 4-5 (sole possession of first place, NFC South)

Saints record: 3-6

Panthers penalties: 17 accepted, 152 yards (franchise record)

Charlie Smyth: 55-yard FG to tie it at 0:41 (longest of his young career)

Alvin Kamara: 3-yard TD run (Saints' only lead)

Ryan Fitzgerald: 5 career game-winners (franchise record, first two years)

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:57 PM ET

Jaxon Smith-Njigba goes for 171 as the Seahawks spoil Fernando Mendoza's first start

By Matt

Jaxon Smith-Njigba had one catch at halftime. He finished with 171 yards and the game-breaking touchdown — and Seattle's backup quarterback and defense did the rest in a 21-13 win over the Raiders.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The day

The Seattle Seahawks beat the Las Vegas Raiders 21-13 at Allegiant Stadium on Sunday, spoiling the first NFL start of No. 1 pick Fernando Mendoza. Jaxon Smith-Njigba caught eight passes for 171 yards and a touchdown on 10 targets, including a 71-yard score. The touchdown came from backup quarterback Drew Lock, who went 14-of-17 for 240 yards with two touchdowns, no interceptions and a 158.0 passer rating — but lost two fumbles. Sam Darnold was out with ribs and quad injuries for a second straight week. Mendoza finished 17-of-29 for 143 yards and was sacked six times. Zach Charbonnet carried 20 times for 94 yards.

A quiet first half, then the third quarter

Smith-Njigba had exactly one catch for 11 yards entering halftime, and Seattle trailed 10-7. The Raiders had struck first after Lock fumbled on the game's second play from scrimmage, and Mendoza capped an 11-play drive with a 10-yard touchdown run on third-and-goal. Lock answered with a 34-yard touchdown pass to Cooper Kupp, but a Matt Gay field goal sent Las Vegas into the break ahead.

Then the third quarter happened. Smith-Njigba piled up 144 yards on six catches in the quarter alone — more than the Raiders' entire offense managed in the second half. Seattle scored on its first two drives after the break and never looked back.

The 71-yarder

The game-breaker came on third-and-3 midway through the third quarter. Lock escaped pressure, fired deep over the middle, and Smith-Njigba did the rest, out-running the Raiders defense for a 71-yard touchdown that made it 21-10. It was the second long scoring throw of the day for Lock after scrambling to buy time — and it was Smith-Njigba's 10th touchdown of the season, matching his career high.

The go-ahead score had come one drive earlier, when rookie Jadarian Price ran in a 12-yard touchdown to cap an 87-yard march. Lock hit Smith-Njigba twice for 53 yards on that drive.

The defense closed the door

Seattle's league-leading defense took the game over after halftime. The Raiders went three-and-out on five straight possessions to open the second half, managing minus-17 yards of total offense in that stretch, and punted seven times in a row before finally adding a late field goal. Las Vegas finished with 163 total yards — 27 of them after halftime — and the six sacks kept Mendoza under constant pressure. A second Lock fumble in the fourth quarter kept the Raiders alive, but Gay's 43-yarder with 2:04 left only cut it to eight, and a 15-yard naked bootleg by Lock helped Seattle run out the clock.

What it means

Seattle heads into its bye week at 6-4, winners of three of four, while Las Vegas fell to 4-6. Smith-Njigba's season total now leads the league at 1,207 receiving yards, and he has seven games this season with 100-plus yards and a touchdown — matching Randy Moss in 2007 for the most such games through the first 10 games of a season. He is also just the third player ever, along with Tory Holt in 2003 and Kupp in 2021, to record at least 75 catches, 1,100 yards and 10 touchdowns in his team's first 10 games.

Lock's 158.0 rating was the highest of his career and the second-highest in franchise history. For Mendoza, the debut still had bright spots: the Heisman winner who led Indiana to a national title was 11-of-13 for 90 yards in the first half and scored on the ground. He got the start after Las Vegas traded Kirk Cousins to Minnesota on November 10. Jason Myers missed a 44-yard field-goal attempt in the fourth quarter that would have stretched Seattle's lead.

By the numbers

Seahawks record: 6-4 (into the bye week)

Raiders record: 4-6

Raiders total offense: 163 yards (27 in the second half)

Raiders second-half drives: Five straight three-and-outs to open the half

Cooper Kupp: 34-yard TD catch from Lock

Jadarian Price: 12-yard TD run (first of his NFL career)

Matt Gay: FGs of 34 and 43 yards

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:57 PM ET

Trevor Lawrence throws for 352 and three scores as the Jaguars hang 40 on Tennessee

By Matt

Trevor Lawrence and Parker Washington took over in Nashville — a career day for Washington, a 20,000-yard milestone for Lawrence, and a 40-23 Jaguars win that keeps Jacksonville alone atop the AFC South.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The day

The Jacksonville Jaguars beat the Tennessee Titans 40-23 at Nissan Stadium on Sunday, and the passing game did almost all of the damage. Trevor Lawrence finished 25-of-35 for 352 yards with three touchdowns and one interception (a 120.2 passer rating), adding 27 yards on four carries. Parker Washington caught seven passes for 152 yards and a touchdown on nine targets, with a long gain of 50. Jacksonville never trailed.

A first half for the record books

Washington did nearly all of his damage before halftime. He had six catches for 148 yards in the first half alone — a career high for a half, and more than Tennessee's entire passing offense had managed by the break. His two-yard touchdown catch on the game's opening drive made it 7-0, and he had a direct hand in all 16 of Jacksonville's first-half points: a 20-yard toe-tap catch to set up a Cam Little field goal, a 50-yard catch-and-run to set up another, and a 32-yard grab on the final drive of the half to set up a third.

Lawrence was just as hot early, throwing for 234 yards in the first half — a career high for a half. Jacksonville led 16-10 at the break, and the lead never really felt threatened again.

A milestone and a small club

The yardage pushed Lawrence past 20,000 career passing yards. It also put him in select company for the season: he is only the fifth quarterback this year to throw for 300-plus yards and three touchdowns while completing at least 70 percent of his passes. The one blemish was a tipped-ball interception.

The supporting cast

Bhayshul Tuten ran for 88 yards on 26 carries and scored the game-sealing touchdown, adding 25 receiving yards for 113 scrimmage yards. Cam Little kicked four field goals, from 46, 48, 36 and 40 yards. The other two touchdown passes went to Brenton Strange, a three-yarder on fourth-and-2 that stretched the lead to 23-13, and rookie Josh Cameron, a 25-yarder set up by Lawrence's 15-yard scramble on third-and-11.

Tennessee got a solid day from Cam Ward, who completed 28 of 39 passes for 231 yards and two touchdowns with an interception, but the Titans were outgained 459-327 and never got the stops they needed. The win is Jacksonville's third straight, and at 7-2 the Jaguars sit alone on top of the AFC South. Tennessee fell to 1-8 and 0-5 at home.

By the numbers

Jaguars record: 7-2 (three straight wins)

Titans record: 1-8 (0-5 at home)

Total yardage: Jacksonville 459, Tennessee 327

Bhayshul Tuten: 26 car, 88 yds, 1 TD; 113 scrimmage yds

Cam Little: 4 FG (46, 48, 36, 40)

Cam Ward: 28/39, 231 yds, 2 TD, 1 INT

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:54 PM ET

Drake Maye leaves Munich game with an ankle injury — and Hutchinson hurt his knee on the same play

By Matt

One sack on the opening drive in Munich cost both teams a star: Drake Maye left with an ankle injury and never returned, and Aidan Hutchinson hurt his knee celebrating the play. New England lost 35-10 to Detroit.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The play

Five and a half minutes into Sunday's game in Munich, the Patriots lost their quarterback — and the Lions lost their best pass rusher on the very same snap.

On third-and-goal on New England's opening drive, Aidan Hutchinson beat tackle Will Campbell, caught Drake Maye from behind, and dragged him down. Maye's right ankle appeared to get caught underneath Hutchinson's body as he went to the turf. Maye was doubled over on the sideline after the play while the Patriots kicked a field goal, then walked gingerly to the locker room. He never returned, and New England ruled him out with about six minutes left in the first half.

Before the injury, Maye had completed 3 of 4 passes for 48 yards on the drive and added an 11-yard scramble. The Patriots managed just 151 total yards the rest of the way in a 35-10 loss to Detroit, while the Lions racked up 494.

The other half of the play

Hutchinson never got to enjoy the sack. He leapt in the air to celebrate, and when he landed his knee seemed to buckle — he recoiled immediately, collapsed, and grabbed his right knee before heading to the locker room for evaluation. Detroit ruled him out in the third quarter, while leading 28-3. A person with knowledge of the situation later told the Associated Press that Hutchinson suffered a season-ending knee injury.

No flag was thrown on the play. Fox rules analyst Mike Pereira said Hutchinson was not guilty of a hip-drop tackle, and Patriots coach Mike Vrabel said he did not consider the sack improper. "I'm not going to assume there was something there," Vrabel said. "However it happened, I don't think it was anything intentional. I'm sure that it wasn't. That's part of the game."

What Vrabel said

Vrabel had little to add on Maye's prognosis after the game. "Obviously wasn't able to finish the game, so we'll have to get some more tests run," he said at his postgame news conference, adding that the team would see "when we come back what it ends up being and the severity of whatever injury he has." He called it "a low leg injury," and said Maye "couldn't play, couldn't run, couldn't do whatever he needed to do to play quarterback, to protect himself, to execute, or function at that level."

That is all that is known for now. There is no official diagnosis beyond the ankle injury, and no timeline.

Life without the QB1

Losing Maye — the 2025 MVP runner-up, who had not missed a start since becoming New England's starter in Week 6 of the 2024 season — flattened the Patriots' offense. Backup Tommy DeVito completed 12 of 24 passes for 81 yards with one touchdown and one interception, and the drop-off was immediate: before halftime he went 4 of 9 for 16 yards with a pick, the Patriots punted on four straight drives, and the offense totaled 29 yards on 20 plays with him at quarterback. Thirty of those yards came on a defensive pass interference penalty — without it, the total was minus-1. New England managed 91 yards of offense after Maye's injury.

DeVito did at least get the Patriots into the end zone early in the fourth quarter, finishing a 13-play, 87-yard drive with a 10-yard touchdown pass to Lan Larison for the rookie's first NFL score. "We never got to establish who we were," DeVito said afterward. "Kind of felt like we were playing on their terms, and I think that's just not our style of football."

DeVito became Maye's primary backup this year after the Patriots claimed him off waivers before the 2025 season and signed him to a two-year, $7.4 million deal in March. He had not taken a snap in 2025 and had thrown eight passes in two brief appearances this season before Sunday.

The loss drops New England to 5-5, with back-to-back defeats and three losses in its last four. The timing at least offers some breathing room: the Patriots head into their bye week, then visit the Los Angeles Chargers on Nov. 29 in Week 12.

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:54 PM ET

Jahmyr Gibbs breaks Terrell Davis's touchdown record in Munich — 252 scrimmage yards in a Lions rout

By Matt

Jahmyr Gibbs needed two touchdowns in Munich to pass Terrell Davis. He got both, piled up 252 yards from scrimmage, and gave Detroit a 35-10 win over New England in front of 70,225 fans at Allianz Arena.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Jahmyr Gibbs walked into Allianz Arena one touchdown short of history and walked out owning it outright. The Detroit Lions running back scored twice in a 35-10 rout of the New England Patriots on Sunday in Munich, and the second of those scores gave him 62 career touchdowns — the most by any player through his first four NFL seasons. He did it while putting up 252 yards from scrimmage, in front of 70,225 fans, as Detroit won its fifth straight game.

The record falls

The mark had belonged to Terrell Davis, the Hall of Fame Denver Broncos running back and two-time Super Bowl champion, whose 61 touchdowns through four seasons had stood alone for nearly three decades. Gibbs arrived in Munich sitting on 60 career scores — 48 on the ground and 12 as a receiver — since the Lions drafted him 12th overall in 2023.

The first of his two scores drew him level with Davis. It came on a 5-yard run late in the second quarter, with 2:06 left in the half, and it capped a drive that was almost entirely his own doing: a 50-yard catch-and-run on a third-and-10 dump-off set it up on the previous snap, and he accounted for every yard of the march before punching it in to make it 21-3.

The breaking run had to wait until the fourth quarter. New England had finally reached the end zone, trimming the lead to 28-10, and Gibbs answered almost immediately — breaking free on a counter to the right, with Penei Sewell pulling in front of him, and going the distance with 8:56 left to make it 35-10 and put the record in his hands alone, with eight games still left in his fourth season.

The line

Rushing: 22; 153; 2 (long 28)

Receiving: 7 (8 targets); 99; 0

Most of the damage came before halftime. Gibbs had 171 yards from scrimmage by the break — the most by a Lions running back in a first half in franchise history, a franchise that has featured Barry Sanders. He touched the ball 29 times all day, averaging roughly 8.7 yards every time he did, and the performance was his second-most productive day as a pro. It beat his previous season high, 207 yards at Arizona in Week 5, by 45.

The afternoon also put him over a round number on the season. The breaking touchdown run pushed him to exactly 1,000 rushing yards, on 199 carries, and made him the first player in NFL history with at least 1,000 rushing yards and 350 receiving yards through his team's first nine games of a season.

The company he keeps

The new career total ties Gibbs for third-most touchdowns in Lions franchise history, trailing only Barry Sanders (109) and Calvin Johnson (84). He is also just the third player ever to reach 50 rushing touchdowns before turning 25, joining Sanders and Emmitt Smith.

Sanders himself was watching from the sideline in Munich, and he was full of praise when sideline reporter Pam Oliver caught up with him after the half. "He's been phenomenal," Sanders said of Gibbs. "He's really established himself as one of the best runners in the game, and I think that this team is going to go as far as Jahmyr is going to take them." He added that Gibbs has already broken a few of his records — and will break more.

The game around him

Gibbs was not the only Lion who owned the afternoon. Jared Goff threw for 303 yards and three touchdowns against two interceptions, and Amon-Ra St. Brown caught four passes for 98 yards and two scores, including a 56-yarder on Detroit's opening drive. Goff's other touchdown went to Isaac TeSlaa on a 12-yard pass early in the second quarter.

St. Brown's day carried its own meaning. His mother is from Leverkusen, and this was his first NFL game in Germany — he ran out for introductions carrying a German flag, then scored twice in front of a crowd that chanted Goff's name, did the wave in the third quarter, and roared at every big play. It was Detroit's first international game in 11 years.

The win moved the Lions to 7-2 and the top of the NFC North. They head back to Detroit to host the Tampa Bay Buccaneers next Sunday at Ford Field.
