Collectibles · Nov 15, 2026 · 9:10 AM ET

Minamo, School at Water's Edge jumped 50% this week

By Matt

The Tales of Middle-earth Commander printing of Minamo, School at Water's Edge was Magic's biggest riser in Cardometer's week-46 report, up 50% to €1,717.50 — while the card's cheapest copies were actually falling ahead of its Star Trek reprint.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The week's biggest riser

Minamo, School at Water's Edge was the biggest riser in Magic: The Gathering in Cardometer's week-46 market report, covering November 9–15, 2026. The Tales of Middle-earth Commander printing climbed 50% against its 7-day average and now sits at €1,717.50.

The move is measured against the 7-day average of the Cardmarket trend, and the report covers 307,050 priced cards across 19 catalogues. Cardometer excludes cards under €5.00 and jumps above 50% — the kind of spike that usually traces back to a single sale — so this one landed exactly on the line.

The other side of the week

The biggest drop went the other way just as hard. Dragonlord Silumgar from Double Masters 2022 fell 50% in the same report, down to €5.16. One card's week, two opposite directions: a scarce Commander printing spiking, a recent Masters reprint sliding.

Why this printing, why now

Minamo is a legendary land from Champions of Kamigawa, illustrated by Jeremy Jarvis. It taps for blue mana with no drawback and untaps a legendary permanent for a single blue — a combination that has kept it a Commander staple for more than two decades.

The price action is about scarcity, not the card in general. This printing was a box topper in Tales of Middle-earth Commander, and Minamo had only ever been reprinted in The List and as that box topper before this week. So when demand ticks up, there is almost no supply to absorb it.

Tales of Middle-earth Commander (box topper): €1,717.50, up 50% this week

Cheapest printing overall: About $21.76 and falling

That split is the real story. The same week the box-topper printing jumped, the card's cheapest copies were sliding — Multiverse Dispatch tracked the cheapest printing falling from $56.53 to $23.74 on TCGplayer in the week ending November 11, below its low of the preceding month.

The reason is sitting on shelves right now. Minamo was just reprinted as Academy on the Bay in the Star Trek Stardates bonus sheet — 30 mythic reprints that launched with the Star Trek set on November 13, 2026. A fresh printing takes pressure off the cheapest copies, and the market is repricing accordingly. The old scarce printing, meanwhile, is moving on its own track: collectors chasing the original, not players chasing a copy to sleeve up.

What to watch

If you own the Tales of Middle-earth Commander printing, this is the kind of week that rewards holding and watching rather than rushing to list — a 50% weekly jump on a thinly traded printing can retrace fast. If you just want the card to play, the Stardates version is the cheap route in, and its price is still settling after release week.

TSL Bets · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:16 AM ET

TSL Bets parlay: Cook OVER 91.5 rushing + Kittle OVER 61.5 receiving (~+255)

By Matt

Two props, two stars, two bad defenses. James Cook runs at a Jets front giving up 128 yards a game on the ground, and George Kittle goes at a banged-up Cowboys secondary while chasing Terrell Owens on the 49ers' all-time list. Cook OVER 91.5 + Kittle OVER 61.5 pays about +255.

The bet

James Cook OVER 91.5 rushing yards (-113) + George Kittle OVER 61.5 receiving yards (-113) — two-leg parlay, about +255

Leg 1 — James Cook OVER 91.5 rushing yards

### The game slip

Buffalo Bills (7-1) at New York Jets (2-7)

Sunday, 1:00 PM ET — MetLife Stadium — CBS

• Spread: Bills -7.5

• Odds source: OddsLive

Injury report — Bills: OT Spencer Brown DOUBTFUL (ankle), CB Christian Benford questionable (wrist).

Injury report — Jets: LB Trevin Wallace OUT (groin), WR Garrett Wilson questionable (concussion) but expected back, WR Adonai Mitchell questionable (knee).

Storylines: The Bills have the No. 1 scoring offense and the No. 2 rushing offense (146.6 yards per game). The Jets are 2-7 and give up 128.0 rushing yards per game, 26th in the league — three rushers have already cleared 100 against them.

### Why Cook

Cook is having a career year: 814 yards on 169 carries, 101.8 per game, 8 rushing touchdowns, and a seven-game touchdown streak. Last week he carried 31 times for 107 yards against Minnesota. And the last time he faced the Jets, he ran for 132 yards and two touchdowns.

The matchup is the bet: a top-two rushing offense against a bottom-six run defense, with a back who's averaging over 100 a game.

### The honest caveat

Blowout risk. The Bills are 7.5-point favorites, and in last season's 35-6 blowout of the Jets, Cook got just two carries. If Buffalo is up three scores at halftime, the starters sit. The Jets also lead the league in forced fumbles (13), so ball security matters.

Leg 2 — George Kittle OVER 61.5 receiving yards

### The game slip

San Francisco 49ers (7-1) at Dallas Cowboys (4-5)

Sunday, 4:25 PM ET — AT&T Stadium — FOX

• Spread: 49ers -4.5

• Total: 55.5

• Moneyline: 49ers -218 / Cowboys +180

• Odds source: OddsLive

Injury report — 49ers: WR Brandon Aiyuk OUT, WR Chris Evans OUT, WR Ricky Pearsall on IR, OT Trent Williams questionable (shoulder), all three starting cornerbacks questionable. Deebo Samuel RETURNS.

Injury report — Cowboys: TE Jake Ferguson OUT, WR Jonathan Mingo OUT, DE Donovan Ezeiruaku OUT (ankle), CBs DaRon Bland and Shavon Revel Jr. questionable.

Storylines: The 49ers are 7-1 and the highest-scoring team in the league, and they're short at receiver — which means the middle of the field is Kittle's. And there's a milestone in play: Kittle (8,491 career receiving yards) needs 82 to pass Terrell Owens on the 49ers' all-time list.

### Why Kittle

Kittle has 38 catches for 483 yards and 5 touchdowns (60.4 per game), and he's gone over 61.5 in five of eight games. His last three weeks: 88 at Atlanta, 58 against Las Vegas, 69 against Washington — a 71.7-yard average, targeted 8.3 times a game over that stretch. The projection models like him too: OPTIMAL projects 69.0 yards.

With Aiyuk out, Evans out, and Pearsall on IR, the 49ers' passing game runs through Kittle and the returning Deebo. The Cowboys are down a tight end and two corners. The line sits right at his season average — and he's been above it in three straight weeks.

### The honest caveat

Deebo's return could eat targets, and 61.5 is Kittle's season average, not a discount. This leg is the steadier of the two, not a lock.

The record

TSL Bets is 18-31 in November, 22-52 all-time. Two-leggers have paid about +250 when they hit — this one pays about +255, right in the zone.

TSL Bets · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:16 AM ET

TSL Bets: Jaylen Warren OVER 61.5 rushing yards vs the Bengals

By Matt

The Steelers are off a bye, the Bengals have the worst run defense in football, and Jaylen Warren already ran for 127 on them once this season. The play: Warren OVER 61.5 rushing yards (-114) on Sunday Night Football.

The game slip

Pittsburgh Steelers (6-2) at Cincinnati Bengals (6-2)

Sunday, 8:20 PM ET — Paycor Stadium, Cincinnati — NBC

• Spread: Bengals -3.5

• Total: 49.5

• Moneyline: Steelers +155 / Bengals -178

• Odds source: BettorsInsider, November 14

Injury report — Steelers: TE Pat Freiermuth QUESTIONABLE (illness, added Saturday), DT Derrick Harmon questionable (foot).

Injury report — Bengals: S Kyle Dugger OUT (quadriceps), WR Andrei Iosivas doubtful, CB Jalen Davis doubtful, G Brian Parker II doubtful.

Storylines: The AFC North lead is on the line in primetime — both teams are 6-2. The Steelers come off a bye, rested and healthy at the skill spots. And the matchup is the whole bet: Warren against a Bengals run defense that has been the worst in football all season.

The bet

Jaylen Warren OVER 61.5 rushing yards (-114, FanDuel)

DraftKings lists the same bet at 63.5-64.5 — shop for the 61.5 at FanDuel if you can get it.

Why this prop

Warren is the most efficient runner in the league right now: 704 yards on 119 carries, 88.0 yards per game, 5.9 yards per carry — the best mark in the NFL at 5.92 per FantasyLabs. His last five rushing lines: 93, 101, 50, 101, 143. He's cleared 64.5 in five of his last six, has four 100-yard games, and has gone over 13.5 carries in five straight.

And he's already done it to this team. In Week 3, Warren ran for 127 yards on 17 carries against these same Bengals — plus 49 receiving yards — in a 30-27 Steelers win.

Cincinnati's run defense is the worst in the league by the numbers that matter: a league-worst 5.04 yards per carry allowed, 27th in EPA against the run. The Bengals are third in run-stuff rate and their defensive tackle Dexter Lawrence ranks 10th in run-stop win rate, so it's not a free 130 — but the volume is there, the matchup is there, and the Steelers are coming off a bye with a fresh lead back.

The honest caveats

Rico Dowdle is back in the Bengals' backfield, which could keep Cincinnati's offense on the field longer — and if Joe Burrow (2,386 passing yards, 23 touchdowns) gets hot early, Pittsburgh may have to throw more than run. Aaron Rodgers (1,871 yards, 13 touchdowns) has to keep the Steelers in the game for Warren to get his carries. But at 61.5, with the 63.5-64.5 available everywhere else, this is the number to play.

The record

TSL Bets is 18-31 in November, 22-52 all-time. Props are 5-1 in November — the only winning singles category — that's why the daily single is a prop.

Collectibles · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:15 AM ET

Magic is bringing back the Black Lotus — nerfed, underwater, and limited to about 500 copies

By Matt

Wizards of the Coast revealed Abyssal Lotus, the headliner chase card of Nauctis: The Sunken Realm — a nerfed Black Lotus that costs nothing, taps for three mana of any color, and only works if you have eight permanents in your graveyard. The special gold-trim version: roughly 500 copies, Collector Boosters only.

The card

The most famous card in Magic: The Gathering history is coming back — with a catch, and a depth charge.

Wizards of the Coast revealed Abyssal Lotus at MagicCon Atlanta, the headliner chase card of Nauctis: The Sunken Realm, the first set of 2027 (releasing February 5, with prerelease on January 29). It's a nerfed Black Lotus: a 0-cost artifact that exiles itself to add three mana of any one color — but it enters tapped, and it only works if you have eight or more permanent cards in your graveyard (the set's new Descend 8 mechanic).

The original Black Lotus, from Alpha in 1993, taps for three mana of any color with no restrictions at all — and it has never had an official tournament-legal reprint since Unlimited in 1993, which is why real copies sell for tens to hundreds of thousands of dollars. The Abyssal Lotus keeps the dream alive at a fraction of the power: the same three mana, the same zero cost, but gated behind a graveyard you have to work for, and it comes in tapped.

The set

Nauctis is an underwater plane — Merfolk, seals, sharks, and a Whale God named Salinae the Ocean Mother — and it's the first set with official "god packs" under a new booster framework. The Abyssal Lotus comes in two showcase treatments: a Coral Frame version and a textless, gold-trim headliner that appears only in Collector Boosters, with roughly 500 copies of that special treatment in existence. Wizards also previewed 19 cards at MagicCon, including Ring of Unity (artifacts tap for two colorless) and a Crucible of Worlds reprint. One quirk of the plane: there's no Flying in the set.

Five hundred copies of the gold-trim headliner. One nerfed Lotus. A lot of people are about to spend a lot of money on Collector Boosters.

Entertainment · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:15 AM ET

Phil Collins cried, Oprah surprised everyone, and Wu-Tang brought Staten Island to the Rock Hall

By Matt

The Rock & Roll Hall of Fame's Class of 2026 took over the Peacock Theater in Los Angeles on Saturday — Phil Collins, 75, weeping at a standing ovation on two canes; Oprah Winfrey's unannounced induction of Luther Vandross; and Wu-Tang Clan, all ten of them, closing the night out with the room on its feet.

The night

The Rock & Roll Hall of Fame induction ceremony returned to Los Angeles on Saturday night, and the Peacock Theater got one of the most emotional nights in the Hall's history.

Phil Collins, 75, was the center of it. Inducted as a solo artist — he was already in the Hall as a member of Genesis, in 2010 — Collins took the stage slowly, on two canes, and cried at the ovation that greeted him. Travis Barker inducted him, praising his unprecedented dual stardom as a drummer and a hit-making pop singer in the 1980s, and Cynthia Erivo sang a slowed-down "In the Air Tonight" while Barker played the drum fill. Collins hasn't performed since 2022, and his daughter Lily Collins was in the crowd. His message to the room: you haven't heard the last of me.

The surprise

Oprah Winfrey's appearance was unannounced. She took the stage to induct Luther Vandross, who died in 2005, calling him "the maestro of the love song" — and noting, with relish, that he was once correctly named "the top artist that people make love to." Usher sang a medley of "Here and Now" and "So Amazing" in tribute.

The party

Wu-Tang Clan was inducted by Chris Rock, all ten current members on stage together, and turned the ceremony into a celebration — a medley of "C.R.E.A.M.," "Method Man," and Ol' Dirty Bastard's "Shimmy Shimmy Ya" that had the crowd on its feet. "We took Staten Island to the world," RZA said.

Queen Latifah and MC Lyte entered as Early Influence inductees, alongside Gram Parsons, Fela Kuti, and Celia Cruz — Megan Thee Stallion inducted Latifah and performed "U.N.I.T.Y." and "Ruffneck." U2 opened the night as surprise guests with a new single, "Silencio," performed with riot-shield dancers, then played "Pride (In the Name of Love)." Joy Division and New Order were inducted together, with Peter Hook performing alongside Deftones and Lauren Mayberry, and Rick Rubin was inducted with the Red Hot Chili Peppers dressed as him. Run-DMC and Aerosmith closed the show with "Walk This Way."

Not everyone made it: Oasis were no-shows (Noel Gallagher was gigging in London, Liam declined), Sade didn't attend (inducted by Andra Day, with Sienna Spiro singing "Smooth Operator"), and Iron Maiden were touring Australia.

The edited broadcast airs on ABC and Disney+ on December 9. The Class of 2026 spans 18 artists and figures — and Saturday night proved the Hall still knows how to put on a show.

Gaming · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:15 AM ET

Wolfenstein 3 is real — and id Software has a new game coming too

By Matt

At Xbox's 25th anniversary FanFest, CEO Asha Sharma confirmed what Wolfenstein fans have waited a decade to hear: Wolfenstein 3 is in production at MachineGames — announced with a photo of the script's first page. And id Software has a new game in the works, while DOOM's Slaughter Mode lands Monday.

The announcement

No trailer. No logo. No release date. Just a photo of a script page on a clipboard — and it was enough to break the internet for Wolfenstein fans.

At the closing show of Xbox's 25th anniversary FanFest in Seattle on Wednesday, Xbox CEO Asha Sharma confirmed that Wolfenstein 3 is in production at MachineGames. The announcement came the way only MachineGames would do it: the studio posted a photo of the script's first page, headed "Chapter 01 – Nazitraz," opening on an interrogation cell where B.J. Blazkowicz leans in close to a prisoner named Otto.

It's the first official word on a third mainline Wolfenstein game since The New Colossus shipped in 2017. Brian Bloom returns as B.J. Blazkowicz and Debra Wilson returns as Grace Walker, and the script page suggests Fergus Reid is back in the mix too. That's it for now — no subtitle, no release window, no platforms, no footage. Just a page, a chapter title, and a room full of fans who'd waited nine years to hear it.

The context

The timing is no accident. Xbox turned 25 on November 15, and Sharma — who took over as Xbox CEO in February after Phil Spencer's retirement — used the anniversary show to make the announcement herself. Wolfenstein is one of Bethesda's crown jewels, and MachineGames has spent the years since The New Colossus on the Indiana Jones game and its DLC. The third Blazkowicz game was the one thing the studio hadn't said out loud. Now it has.

Also from the show: id Software's next game, and DOOM's Slaughter Mode

Wolfenstein wasn't the only Bethesda news. id Software confirmed it has a new game in development, with teams in Texas and Frankfurt and game direction once again by Hugo Martin — no title, no date, no genre details yet. And DOOM: The Dark Ages gets its Slaughter Mode on Monday, November 16: an endless combat challenge with randomized waves across 19 arenas and a Skulls reward system, free for owners of the Revelations DLC, the Premium Edition, or the Premium Upgrade. Base-game and base Game Pass owners don't get it — the mode is tied to the expansion.

NHL · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:13 AM ET

The Avalanche are missing MacKinnon and Makar — and they just ended Montreal's 12-game point streak anyway

By Matt

No Nathan MacKinnon. No Cale Makar. No problem. The Avalanche walked into Bell Centre and beat the Canadiens 4-1, ending Montreal's 12-game point streak and handing the Habs their first regulation loss since October 14.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Colorado Avalanche showed up in Montreal on Saturday night without their two best players — and handed the Canadiens their worst result in a month.

Colorado beat Montreal 4-1 at Bell Centre, ending the Canadiens' 12-game point streak (9-0-3) and handing them their first regulation loss since October 14, a 4-3 defeat at Washington. The Avs did it without Nathan MacKinnon, out at least a month, and Cale Makar, out roughly two weeks — both sidelined with lower-body injuries suffered in the second period of Thursday's 4-3 overtime loss to Ottawa.

How it happened

The Avalanche's depth did the work. Matvei Svechkov opened the scoring at 3:57 of the first, Brock Nelson — who took MacKinnon's place on the top line — added a goal, and Artturi Lehkonen scored his team-leading 15th of the season (a power-play goal on his own rebound at 4:14 of the third, his third straight game with a goal) to make it 3-1. Nicolas Roy's empty-netter finished it off.

Mackenzie Blackwood made 25 saves behind them, and the Avs stretched their own point streak to five games (3-0-2) at 12-6-3. Colorado's next-man-up act extended to the blue line too: Sam Malinski stepped onto the top pair alongside Devon Toews and logged 24:19 with an assist.

Montreal's lone goal was worth the price of admission — captain Nick Suzuki beat Blackwood with a spin-o-rama at 10:25 of the second. But the Canadiens, already short-handed (Chris Kreider is out six to eight weeks with a lower-body injury suffered Thursday against Boston), couldn't solve Blackwood again. Jakub Dobes made 22 saves in the loss.

What it means

A point streak is a fragile thing — 12 games of never leaving the rink empty-handed, undone in one night by a team missing its two best players. Montreal (12-4-4) still has plenty of cushion, and the Habs get a quick chance to start a new one: they visit the Rangers on Monday night. Colorado heads to Utah on Wednesday, still without its two stars, still winning.

Cutter Gauthier's goal streak hits eight games — 15 goals in eight games, and the Ducks still lost

By Matt

Cutter Gauthier scored his 17th goal of the season in Anaheim's 6-2 loss to the Islanders, extending his goal streak to eight games and his point streak to ten. Fifteen goals in eight games, three hat tricks in the run — and the Ducks lost by four anyway.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Cutter Gauthier cannot stop scoring. The Anaheim Ducks cannot stop losing anyway.

Gauthier scored his 17th goal of the season on Saturday night in a 6-2 loss to the New York Islanders, extending his goal streak to eight games and his point streak to ten. He has scored 15 goals during the eight-game streak — including three hat tricks — which means he has scored nearly as many goals in the last eight games as most players score in a season.

The goal came with 3:23 left in the second period, a small bit of light in a game that was already getting away from Anaheim. Simon Holmstrom recorded his first career hat trick for the Islanders, Matthew Schaefer added three assists, and New York pulled away to earn at least a point for the fourth consecutive game.

The run

This is the second time in a week Gauthier has forced his way into this recap. Last Sunday he scored his third hat trick in five games — the first player in NHL history with three hat tricks in the first 10 days of a calendar month — and had 13 goals in a six-game goal streak. A week later the numbers are somehow better: 15 goals in eight games, 17 on the season, and a point streak that's now at ten.

The context that makes it stranger is the team around him. Anaheim lost 6-2 on a night its star scored again, and the Ducks are still just trying to keep up with him.

The Ducks host Calgary on Sunday. Gauthier goes for nine straight.

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:10 AM ET

Kayla Harrison chokes out Amanda Nunes in the fourth — the GOAT's comeback ends in a tap

By Matt

Amanda Nunes came out of a three-year retirement to take her belt back. Kayla Harrison caught a kick in the fourth round, took her back, and ended the comeback with a rear-naked choke at 1:29 — keeping the women's bantamweight title at UFC 334.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The comeback lasted three rounds and 89 seconds of a fourth.

Kayla Harrison retained the UFC women's bantamweight title on Saturday night at Madison Square Garden, submitting Amanda Nunes with a rear-naked choke at 1:29 of the fourth round in the co-main event of UFC 334 — spoiling the return of the woman widely regarded as the greatest female fighter of all time.

Nunes, the former two-division champion, was fighting for the first time since retiring as champion in June 2023 — more than three years out of the cage. Harrison, the two-time Olympic judo gold medalist, was herself returning from a lengthy layoff after neck surgery. The buildup was the biggest women's fight the UFC could make. The ending was sudden and total.

The finish

Harrison had dominated the fight before the finish. She outstruck Nunes 169 to 62 in total strikes, landing at 88 percent accuracy, and spent long stretches pinning the Brazilian to the canvas and punishing her with ground-and-pound — at one point, two straight minutes of punches to the head while Nunes was flattened out underneath her.

The end came fast. In the fourth, Nunes landed a left hook in the clinch — and Harrison caught a kick, tripped her to the mat, chased her down, took her back, and had the choke in before the two had even really hit the ground. Nunes tapped, and that was that.

Harrison improves to 20-1 (4-0 in the UFC) and takes home a $100,000 Performance of the Night bonus. Nunes falls to 23-6 (16-3 in the UFC), and the question now is whether the greatest women's fighter ever tries again — or whether this was the last time we see her in the cage.

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 8:10 AM ET

Ciryl Gane survives Josh Hokit in a record-breaking war to keep the heavyweight title at UFC 334

By Matt

Ciryl Gane kept his undisputed heavyweight title with a 48-47 unanimous decision over Josh Hokit at Madison Square Garden — a five-round war that set the UFC heavyweight record for combined significant strikes, and left half the building arguing about the scorecards.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Twenty-five minutes, 796 total strikes thrown, and three scorecards that all read the same — and the building still wasn't sure.

Ciryl Gane retained the undisputed UFC heavyweight title on Saturday night at Madison Square Garden, beating previously unbeaten challenger Josh Hokit by unanimous decision (48-47, 48-47, 48-47) in the main event of UFC 334. It was Gane's first defense of the undisputed belt, and it took everything he had.

Hokit, the No. 4 contender, charged forward and tripped Gane seconds into the fight, then spent the early rounds in the champion's face — bloodied Gane's nose with right hands, kept a break-neck pace, and banked the early rounds on pressure and volume. The plan was obvious: make the French technician tired, and take the belt off him late. For three rounds, it looked like it might work.

Then the championship rounds arrived, and Gane did what champions do. He rallied in the fourth with knees to the body as Hokit's pace slowed, and in the fifth he landed the more impactful, significant blows as Hokit emptied out. By the final bell, the two heavyweights had combined to land 541 of those 796 strikes — Gane connecting on 298 of 412, Hokit on 243 of 384 — and set a new UFC heavyweight record for combined significant strikes in a fight.

The argument

The decision itself is the story now. All three judges scored it 48-47 for Gane, but plenty of people watching — including Alex Pereira, cageside, who called it a robbery for Hokit — had the challenger winning. MMA Junkie's own live scoring gave the fight to Hokit, 48-47.

Hokit suffers his first professional loss (now 10-1, 4-1 in the UFC). Gane improves to 15-2 (12-2 in the UFC, with one no-contest). Both men took home $100,000 Fight of the Night bonuses, and both left with something real: Gane keeps the belt, and Hokit — who was supposed to be the underdog in over his head — proved he belongs at the very top of the division.

"All the people around the world underestimated him. Not me. He did so well tonight. It was a tough fight," Gane said afterward.

Also on the card

The undercard delivered its own history. Stephen Thompson closed out his career with a spinning wheel kick TKO of Charles Radtke in the third round of the featured prelim — a retirement fight that ended the only way Wonderboy could end one. Bia Mesquita submitted Macy Chiasson with a rear-naked choke in the first round, breaking Ronda Rousey's record for the most submissions in UFC women's bantamweight history (four, passing Rousey's three) and becoming the first woman in UFC history to win four straight fights by submission. And Luis Gurule, a +700 underdog, took a split decision over previously unbeaten Bilal Hasan.

NHL · Nov 15, 2026 · 7:59 AM ET

Dmitry Orlov's four-point night — and a franchise-record six seconds — carry the Sharks past Utah in a shootout

By Matt

Dmitry Orlov had a goal and three assists, including the go-ahead goal with 5:43 left, as San Jose rallied from two down to beat Utah 6-5 in a shootout — a night that also saw the Sharks score twice in six seconds, a franchise record, and a rookie defenseman score his first NHL goal.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The San Jose Sharks spent most of Saturday night chasing the game. They trailed 2-0, then 3-1, then 4-3. They were outshot 32-21 and didn't hold a lead until Dmitry Orlov's slap shot with 5:43 left in regulation.

Then they won it anyway — 6-5 in a shootout over the Utah Mammoth at Delta Center, in a game that packed a four-point night from a defenseman, a franchise record, and a first NHL goal into one wild evening.

Orlov finished with a goal and three assists, his go-ahead tally at 14:17 of the third a knuckling shot from the point that was partially blocked and sailed over Karel Vejmelka's glove. It was the first lead of the night for San Jose, and it capped a night where the veteran was involved in nearly everything the Sharks did right.

Six seconds of history

The record came in the second period, and it came fast. Adam Gaudette tipped in a point shot from Orlov on the power play at 3:07 to make it 3-2 — and six seconds later, Zack Ostapchuk beat Vejmelka between the legs on the rush off the ensuing faceoff to tie it 3-3. Six seconds apart: the fastest two goals in Sharks franchise history.

The firsts kept coming. Rookie defenseman Eric Pohlkamp, a 2023 fourth-round pick recalled on Nov. 3, scored his first NHL goal at 5:23 of the third — a wrist shot from the top of the slot past Vejmelka's glove — to tie the game 4-4. His defensive partner was the one who made sure he kept the souvenir: Orlov grabbed the puck for him.

"I think Orlov grabbed it for me, which was cool," Pohlkamp said.

Tyler Toffoli added a goal and an assist, Yaroslav Askarov made 27 saves, and after Barrett Hayton tied it 5-5 on a power play at 16:22 and a scoreless overtime, Macklin Celebrini and Mason Marchment scored in the shootout — Marchment's winner coming in the fifth round.

The other side

Utah did plenty right and still lost its fifth straight game. Zac Jones scored twice, Kailer Yamamoto had three assists, Mikhail Sergachev had a goal and an assist, and Jack McBain and Hayton scored — but the Mammoth, playing without Logan Cooley, Nick Schmaltz and Dylan Guenther, couldn't hold three separate leads. Vejmelka stopped 16 of 21 shots.

For San Jose, the win snapped a miserable run — the Sharks had lost six of their past seven (1-5-1) coming in — and it was the kind of win that says something about a young team: they kept coming back, they got the record, they got the rookie his first goal, and they survived the shootout. The Sharks host Nashville on Sunday night.

NHL · Nov 15, 2026 · 7:58 AM ET

Jamie Benn's 1,000th point lifts the Stars past the Sabres — and Oettinger's shutout is the bounce-back

By Matt

Jamie Benn became only the second player in Stars franchise history to record 1,000 points in a Dallas uniform, scoring the winner in a 2-0 blanking of Buffalo — two days after Jake Oettinger was pulled for allowing six goals.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Some milestones arrive with fireworks. Jamie Benn's arrived the way most of his career has gone — in the middle of a scoreless grind, with a captain's goal, in a game his team had to win.

Benn scored late in the second period for the 1,000th point of his 18-year NHL career, and the Dallas Stars beat the Buffalo Sabres 2-0 on Saturday night at American Airlines Center. He joins Hall of Famer Mike Modano (1,359) as the only players in franchise history to record 1,000 points in a Stars uniform.

The goal itself was pure Benn: a snap through traffic with 3:37 left in the second to break a scoreless tie, his fourth goal of the season and third in his last seven games. Wyatt Johnston added a power-play insurance goal with 40 seconds left, on assists from Mikko Rantanen and Roope Hintz, and that was that.

The other half of the story

If Benn's milestone was the headline, the other end of the ice was the redemption. Jake Oettinger turned aside all 22 shots he faced for his fourth shutout of the season and the 20th of his career — two days after he was pulled for allowing six goals on 27 shots in an 8-3 loss to Winnipeg.

It had been a rough stretch for the Dallas netminder: 14 goals allowed in his past three starts before Saturday, and no shutout since Oct. 17, after he'd posted three of them in the team's first six games — including a 4-0 win against these same Sabres on Oct. 8. Saturday was the answer. He denied Noah Ostlund from point-blank range on a power play early in the third to preserve the 1-0 lead, and the Stars killed all five Buffalo power plays on the night.

Dallas outshot Buffalo 30-22, improved to 9-3-1 in its last 13 games, and completed a season sweep of the Sabres. Buffalo's winless streak hit four games, with Alex Lyon making 28 saves in the loss.

The company

The 1,000-point club in Dallas is now two men deep, and the gap between them tells you everything about what Benn has meant to this franchise. Modano's 1,359 points came across 21 seasons and a Stanley Cup. Benn has spent all 18 of his seasons in Dallas, wearing the C, and Saturday's goal put him in the one piece of company in franchise history that matters most.

He didn't make a fuss about it. That's also on brand. The goal came in a 2-0 win that Dallas needed after Thursday's embarrassment in Winnipeg, and the captain treated it like another night at the office — which, for him, it mostly was. The milestone will get its ceremony later. The two points mattered now.

NHL · Nov 15, 2026 · 7:58 AM ET

Daniil Tarasov's 31-save shutout ends Tampa's perfect home record — and Detroit's five-game skid

By Matt

Daniil Tarasov stopped all 31 shots he faced as Detroit blanked Tampa Bay 2-0, handing the Lightning their first home loss of the season and snapping the Red Wings' five-game losing streak in the same night.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

The Tampa Bay Lightning hadn't lost at home all season. The Detroit Red Wings hadn't won in five games. Something had to give on Saturday night at Benchmark International Arena, and Daniil Tarasov made sure it gave Detroit's way.

Tarasov stopped all 31 shots he faced — including a 13-shot second-period barrage — as the Red Wings blanked the Lightning 2-0, becoming the first club to beat Tampa on home ice in 2026-27 and ending a season-long five-game skid in the process.

"We needed this win," Tarasov said. "It was a tough road trip, and we ended it really strong against a really good opponent."

It was the second shutout of the season for Tarasov, and the third of his NHL career. Both of his shutouts in a Winged Wheel sweater have come in the kind of games Detroit desperately needed: the first came in his first start, Oct. 10 at Montreal, and this one arrived with the Wings at 8-10-3 and sliding.

The sequence

The game turned on 31 seconds in the second period.

First, Tarasov made a no-look paddle save on Jake Guentzel at the left post — a lunging stick save that kept the game scoreless. Then William Wallinder, below the Lightning bench, hit Lucas Raymond in the neutral zone with a pass Raymond settled off his skate, kicked to his stick, and converted on the breakaway at 13:12 for the 1-0 lead.

"It was almost like a pass, but a punt as well," coach Todd McLellan said of Wallinder's feed. "And I thought Lucas did a good job of handling that puck in the air. So, the quarterback threw the ball out in an area and let the receiver go get it."

Raymond later added the assist on Michael Brandsegg-Nygård's empty-net goal at 19:17 of the third, sealing it. The 24-year-old now has eight goals and leads Detroit with 20 points through 21 games.

Tarasov's night was full of that kind of moment. In the first period, John Carlson's one-timer slipped behind him but not over the goal line, and Andreas Englund swept the loose puck away from the blue paint. Late in the second, he denied an open Nikita Kucherov one-timer from the high slot. Tampa outshot Detroit 31-22 and kept coming — nine shots in the final frame — but nothing got through.

"Considering some of the past games where I think we've had those types of efforts and they haven't gone our way, it's good to finally get the carrot," McLellan said. "And like a lot of those other games, good team game starting in the goal."

The stakes

For Tampa, it was the first shutout loss of the season and the end of an eight-game home winning streak. The Lightning fell to 13-5-2, with Andrei Vasilevskiy making 20 saves. For Detroit, it was the difference between coming home empty-handed from a four-game road trip and coming home with something to build on. The Wings will play four of their next five at Little Caesars Arena, starting Wednesday against Boston.

The numbers back up what the eye test said: Tarasov is 3-3-2 in nine appearances this season with a 1.84 goals-against average and a .931 save percentage, and he's given a team that came into the night desperate for relief exactly that. Andrew Copp, who picked up an assist on the empty-netter, played his 800th career NHL game in the win.

"We needed this win," Tarasov said again, because it bore repeating. "It was a tough road trip, and we ended it really strong against a really good opponent."

NHL · Nov 15, 2026 · 7:57 AM ET

Devon Levi's first career shutout blanks the Leafs — and McDavid's streak hits 21

By Matt

Devon Levi stopped all 19 shots he faced for his first NHL shutout as Edmonton blanked Toronto 1-0, and Connor McDavid's assist ran his season-opening point streak to 21 games — with 50 points already, the fifth-fastest start to a season in NHL history.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Devon Levi has been dreaming about this one since he was a kid. He said so himself, minutes after it happened.

The 24-year-old stopped all 19 shots he faced for his first career NHL shutout on Saturday night, and the Edmonton Oilers beat the Toronto Maple Leafs 1-0 at Scotiabank Arena to open a six-game road trip with their fourth straight win.

"It feels good, super special," Levi said. "Since I was a kid, I was thinking about getting a shutout in the NHL, so I'm just soaking it in. The guys played unbelievable tonight. I don't know how many shots I saw in the third, but we really locked it down. That's just like a textbook win right there."

The only goal of the night came at 15:34 of the second period, when Evan Bouchard took a backhand pass from Connor McDavid at the point and beat Anthony Stolarz glove side with a slap shot. It was Bouchard's 12th goal of the season — and the assist did something else, too.

The streak

McDavid's helper extended his season-opening point streak to 21 games. He's now one away from tying Dany Heatley's 22-game run in 2005-06 for the fifth-longest season-opening point streak in NHL history.

The raw totals are getting silly. McDavid leads the NHL with 50 points — 11 goals, 39 assists — in 21 games, remaining on pace for 200 points over a full season. He became just the fifth different player in league history to reach 50 points in 21 games or fewer, joining Wayne Gretzky (who did it three times), Mario Lemieux (three times), Bernie Nicholls and Mike Bossy.

"That's pretty good company," Ryan Nugent-Hopkins said. "Not really [do I have the words to put it into perspective], I've seen it for so long and he always finds another level to get to. It's like the older he gets, the more he figures out the game a little bit more. It's hard to wrap your mind around, but he is driving it for us every night."

Leon Draisaitl added an assist of his own to extend his point streak to eight games — six goals and eight assists in that span.

The save that almost wasn't

Toronto thought it had tied the game 1:00 into the third period, when John Tavares appeared to deflect one past Levi. Video review confirmed the deflection came with a stick above the crossbar, and the goal came off the board. Levi said he knew right away.

"I saw the tip and it went over my head, and I thought there's no way the puck should be going over my head like that," he said. "I knew it was a high stick."

The Leafs pulled Stolarz for the extra attacker in the final 1:48, and Auston Matthews had a chance alone in the slot with four seconds left — but shot wide before Nugent-Hopkins blocked the follow-up attempts from William Nylander. Levi's last word on the night was pure goalie: "I saw Matthews in the slot with the puck and I was like, 'Bring it on.' You've got to love it. As a goalie, you always want an opportunity to make a key save at a big moment. That's why I'm there."

The other side

This was a rough one for Toronto, and the sixth straight loss says it plainly enough. The Leafs went winless on their entire six-game homestand, losing each game in regulation while being outscored 23-9, and they've now been shut out twice in the span of a week. Stolarz made 22 saves in his first back-to-back starts of the season, but the Leafs — who routed Edmonton 7-2 on Oct. 24 — couldn't solve the other end.

"It's difficult because I can't just go and tell you all these good things and then we get on the wrong side of it again, so that's the hard part," Toronto coach Jim Hiller said. "The guys played their tail off, they did, so I don't think anybody is going to question that. I'd like to see them get rewarded for that, I really would."

For Levi, the night adds another line to what's becoming a remarkable start to life in Edmonton. Acquired from Buffalo on July 1, he improved to 9-1-0 in 2026-27 — the best 10-game start to a career with the Oilers in franchise history, two wins better than the next closest (Calvin Pickard at 7-2-0 in 2023-24, and Ron Low at 7-2-1 in 1979-80). He didn't play a single NHL game last season. Now he has a shutout, a 9-1 record, and a team that sits at 15-3-3.

Oh, and one more subplot: it was Oilers coach Mike Babcock's first game in Toronto since being fired as Maple Leafs coach on Nov. 20, 2019.

Gear up: Fanatics

Sports · Nov 15, 2026 · 7:56 AM ET

Tyrese Proctor's first triple-double — at any level — lifts the short-handed Cavs past the Mavericks

By Matt

Tyrese Proctor had never recorded a triple-double at any level of organized basketball. Then the Cavaliers rested their entire starting five, handed the 22-year-old Australian his second career start, and watched him put up 24 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists in a 127-117 win over Dallas.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The night

Tyrese Proctor had never done it before. Not in the NBA, not at Duke, not anywhere — the 22-year-old Australian had never recorded a triple-double at any level of organized basketball.

Then the Cavaliers handed him the keys to a team missing its entire starting five, and he went out and got one anyway.

Proctor finished with 24 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists as Cleveland beat the Dallas Mavericks 127-117 on Saturday night at Rocket Arena, in front of a sellout crowd of 19,432 that had come to celebrate the 10-year anniversary of the franchise's 2016 championship and ended up watching the future instead. All three numbers were career marks: the points and assists were career highs, and the rebounds matched his best.

The chaos before tip

The box score only tells half of this one. The day itself was a mess.

Cleveland played in Miami on Friday night, lost 133-121, then got stuck in nationwide travel disruptions and didn't land back in Cleveland until late Saturday morning — a three-hour flight that arrived hours before tip on the second night of a back-to-back. Coach Kenny Atkinson looked at the schedule, looked at the injury report, and sat everybody: Donovan Mitchell (knee), James Harden (calf), Peyton Watson (hamstring) and Jarrett Allen (knee) were all ruled out, and Evan Mobley missed his second straight game with a toe sprain.

That left Jaylon Tyson as the only returning starter from Friday. The other four spots went to reserves — Mario Hezonja and Thomas Bryant making their first starts of the season, Meleek Thomas his third, and Proctor his second. Proctor had started Wednesday's loss to Oklahoma City in place of Watson; this was the first time he'd been asked to run the show.

Dallas, for its part, had no such excuse. The Mavericks were at close to full strength, with Kyrie Irving going for a game-high 34 points and Cooper Flagg — Proctor's former Duke teammate — adding 18 and 8. Cleveland led by 18 at halftime anyway, after a 38-point second quarter, and never really let Dallas back in it. Proctor buried back-to-back threes in the fourth to push the lead to 113-91, the largest of the night.

The line

It wasn't just the counting stats. Proctor shot 10-of-19 from the field and 4-of-13 from three, and he did it while playing the point on a team that had no other ball-handler to lean on. He'd set his previous career high — 22 points — the night before in Miami. Twenty-four hours later he'd beaten it, and added the rebounding and playmaking to turn a good night into a historic one.

Proctor, Nov. 14 vs Dallas: 24; 11; 10

Proctor, previous career highs: 22; 11; 8

He is the first Cavalier with a triple-double since Max Strus did it against Charlotte on April 14, 2024 — more than two and a half years ago. And the "at any level" part is what makes it stick: this is a player who ran the point for three years at Duke and for the Australian national team from the age of 16, and he'd never once finished a game with double figures in three categories. The 49th pick of the 2025 draft, on a four-year, $8.69 million deal, picked the most improbable night of the season to do it for the first time.

Everybody ate

The fun of this game is that Proctor wasn't the only reserve who went off. All five starters scored in double figures — Hezonja had 22 on 9-of-12 shooting, Thomas had 20, Bryant had 19 to go with 11 rebounds of his own, and Tyson had 18 — and Norchad Omier added 13 off the bench. Six Cavaliers in double figures, on a night the team started the day in another city and finished it without a single member of its usual starting five.

That's the part that will sting in Dallas. The Mavericks lost to a team that, on paper, shouldn't have been able to score 127 points. Cleveland shot its way to a 71-53 halftime lead and kept answering every Dallas run, and the win evened the Cavs' record at 7-7. Dallas fell to 7-6, and gets less than a day to think about it — the Mavs play in Boston on Sunday.

What's next

Cleveland has a three-game homestand coming up — the Bulls, Grizzlies and Pacers all visit Rocket Arena this week — and the regulars will be back in the lineup. Proctor will go back to a reserve role, and his minutes will come down. That's fine. Nights like this one are why you keep a 22-year-old second-round pick ready: sometimes the whole starting five sits down, the travel goes sideways, and the kid you drafted 49th runs the show like he's been doing it for years.

TSL Bets: The Saturday parlay (~+449) — Mitchell OVER 14.5 points, Hokit moneyline at UFC 334

By Matt

Davion Mitchell has cleared 14.5 points in 8 of his last 10, and Josh Hokit is a live +180 underdog with the exact wrestling game to attack Ciryl Gane's weakest skill. Two legs, about +449.

Leg 1 — the game

Detroit Pistons (8-3) at Miami Heat (6-6) — 8:00 PM ET, Kaseya Center, Miami, NBA TV

Pistons -4.5, total 229.5 (lines via Nathanite).

Injuries — Detroit: Jalen Duren is out with a right hamstring strain. Isaiah Joe is out with a right hip contusion.

Injuries — Miami: Klay Thompson (ankle) and Pelle Larsson (illness) both sat out Friday; Giannis Antetokounmpo is probable through a wrist issue.

Storylines: Davion Mitchell is running the hottest stretch of his season — 22 points and a career-high-tying 17 assists last night against Cleveland, and he has cleared 14.5 points in 8 of his last 10 games. In the nine games Thompson has missed, Mitchell averages 15.6 points, still above this line. Detroit's defense has to pick its poison between Giannis, Bam and a point guard who just dissected the Cavs.

Leg 2 — the fight

Ciryl Gane (14-2, 1 NC) vs. Josh Hokit (10-0) — UFC heavyweight title, 5 rounds, ~11:30 PM ET, Madison Square Garden, main card 9:00 PM ET on Paramount+

Gane -218, Hokit +180, rounds total 3.5 (over +130, under -166) — DraftKings via ESPN.

Weigh-ins: Gane 242.6 lbs, Hokit 231.4 lbs — both made weight, and all four title-fight fighters are official. Two prelim fighters missed weight; neither is in this fight.

Camp/availability: Hokit trains at Jackson-Wink MMA in New Mexico. Gane became interim champion by stopping Alex Pereira in the second round on June 14, then was promoted to undisputed champion after Tom Aspinall could not return in time to defend. Hokit went from Dana White's Contender Series to a title shot in 53 weeks — he beat Curtis Blaydes by decision and stopped Derrick Lewis by second-round TKO on that same June 14 card.

Storylines: Gane's takedown defense sits at 47%, and both of his career losses came against wrestling: Francis Ngannou out-wrestled him to a decision in January 2022, and Jon Jones submitted him in 2:04 in March 2023. Hokit is a two-time All-American wrestler who finished fifth at the 2019 NCAA Championships at 197 pounds. His camp has said openly the plan is to put Gane on his back and rough him up — the exact thing Jones and Ngannou did. The risk is real too: Hokit has never fought five rounds, Gane owns a 7.5-inch reach advantage, and Hokit absorbs more than twice as many significant strikes per minute as the champion.

The bet

Davion Mitchell OVER 14.5 points (-104, FanDuel) + Josh Hokit moneyline (+180, DraftKings) — about +449. Mitchell's line is the steadier leg: 8 of 10, 4 of his last 5, and a matchup where Miami's short-handed backcourt keeps the ball in his hands. Hokit is the upside leg: a plus-money dog with a real stylistic path, not a lottery ticket — the wrestling edge is documented, the camp is the same one that produced the Jones blueprint, and two former champions have already shown the way to beat Gane. A $10 ticket returns about $54.90.

The honest caveat

Both legs can lose on their own terms. Mitchell is coming off a career night — 22 and 17 — and regression is a real thing; if the Pistons blow Miami out, his minutes shrink. Hokit has to close distance on a superior striker for five rounds without getting picked apart, and his takedown numbers (1.50 per 15 minutes, and 0-for against Blaydes) say the wrestling edge is a plan, not a guarantee. This parlay pays +449 because both things have to happen. That is the deal.

Entertainment · Nov 14, 2026 · 8:04 AM ET

Avril Lavigne is a mom — the pop-punk star announces her first child in a surprise post

By Matt

Avril Lavigne surprised fans on Friday by announcing she has welcomed her first child, a baby girl, sharing three black-and-white photos with her 13.9 million Instagram followers — and no name, no birth date, and no word on who the father is.

The announcement

Avril Lavigne had a secret, and she kept it all the way to the finish line. The 42-year-old singer announced on Instagram on Friday, November 13, that she has welcomed her first child — a baby girl — sharing three black-and-white photos and a caption her fans recognized instantly: "Our Mini F*ing Princess has arrived."

She had never disclosed that she was expecting. No pregnancy announcement, no bump-watch coverage, nothing — the news landed as a complete surprise. The photos show Lavigne cradling her daughter, a close-up of the newborn's hand in hers, and three pairs of checkered Vans on a skateboard. She did not share the baby's name or birth date, did not identify her partner, and disabled comments on the post.

Why it landed

Lavigne has spent her career in the public eye — the "Complicated" and "Sk8er Boi" breakout of 2002, the pop-punk crown, the tabloid years — and this announcement was the opposite of all of that: quiet, private, on her own terms. A representative confirmed the news to ABC News and said she has been enjoying time with her family while continuing to work on a new album.

It is also a reminder of how much the announcement economy has changed. Twenty years ago, a reveal like this would have been a magazine cover negotiated months in advance. On Friday it was three photos, a caption, and 13.9 million followers finding out at the same time as everybody else.

TSL Bets: Giannis OVER 40.5 points + rebounds (-114) vs Pistons — he just cleared it by 5.5 in this exact lineup

By Matt

Giannis Antetokounmpo has cleared 40.5 combined points and rebounds in 8 of his last 10 games — including a 46 last night, in this same short-handed lineup, with Detroit's best rebounder out tonight. Take the over at -114.

The game

Detroit Pistons (8-3) at Miami Heat (6-6) — 8:00 PM ET, Kaseya Center, Miami, NBA TV

Giannis OVER 40.5 points + rebounds (-114). A $10 ticket returns about $18.80.

Collectibles · Nov 14, 2026 · 8:04 AM ET

Costco's Pokémon 30th Anniversary drop sold out in minutes — the ETB bundle went in under 40

By Matt

Costco put a 30th Celebration Elite Trainer Box + Booster Bundle 2-pack online Friday for $125.99 and it was gone in under 40 minutes; the Ascended Heroes mini tins lasted about eight. Membership, a queue, and a price under combined MSRP — the hobby's hottest chase keeps finding new ways to sell out.

The drop

Costco ran two Pokémon drops online on Friday, November 13, and both vanished fast. The headline item was a 30th Celebration Elite Trainer Box + Booster Bundle 2-pack — two ETBs and two booster bundles, 30 packs in all — for $125.99, limit one per membership. It sold out in under 40 minutes. The Ascended Heroes mini tins bundle — 10 tins and 10 promo cards for $86.99 — lasted about eight.

Both drops ran through an online queue, with shoppers placed at random once it went live, and both required a Costco membership. The price is the tell: an ETB alone carries a $49.99 MSRP and a booster bundle runs about $26.99, so two sets at retail would be roughly $154. Costco's $125.99 undercut that by nearly $30 — and the hobby noticed.

The rest of the day

Costco was not the only register ringing. Macy's put up the 30th Celebration Ultra-Premium Collection, the Ditto Premium Collection and the Delta Reign Elite Trainer Box, limit two. Target cycled the 30th Celebration Binder Collection and the Unova Heavy Hitters Premium Collection in and out of stock through the afternoon. Walmart started sending out results from its November 12 drawings for the 30th Celebration UPC, and Cynthia's Garchomp ex Premium Collection was checking out there for $49.97.

Why it keeps happening

The 30th Anniversary line has been the hobby's white whale since the Day and Night Ultra-Premium Collections launched November 6 at $179.99 and started trading at double retail. A week later, Delta Reign singles are spiking while the Star Trek set crashes on release day — and every restock, at every retailer, still clears in minutes. The demand is not cooling. The supply just keeps arriving in drops small enough to disappear.

Gaming · Nov 14, 2026 · 8:03 AM ET

Microsoft's outage reached Xbox — sign-ins, cloud saves and Cloud Gaming all hit, and the status page still says everything is fine

By Matt

A global Microsoft outage that began November 12 knocked out Outlook, Teams and Microsoft 365 — and by Friday, Xbox players were reporting failed sign-ins, games that won't launch and Cloud Gaming queues, even as Xbox's own status page showed all green.

What happened

Microsoft's global outage is on its second day, and it has spread from the office to the living room. The disruption — tracked as incident MO1550821 — began on Thursday, November 12, when the servers behind Exchange Online failed their automated health checks. Outlook, Teams, Copilot, OneDrive and SharePoint went dark for users in 74 countries, with more than 130,000 Outlook problem reports logged at the peak.

By Friday, Xbox players were feeling it too. Users reported failed sign-ins, digital games that would not launch, cloud saves that would not sync, and trouble reaching Xbox Cloud Gaming — long queues, and sessions kicking players out entirely.

The strange part

Here is the part that has players confused: the official Xbox status page lists every service as up and running, including sign-in, multiplayer, the store and cloud gaming. Xbox does not appear on Microsoft's own list of affected products, and Xbox Support has posted nothing about the outage. Microsoft has said a core process on the affected servers became overwhelmed and lost capacity, ruled out malicious activity, and says recovery is ongoing — but has given no time for a full recovery, and its own status page still shows service capacity below normal.

Why the timing stings

The outage landed on an awkward week for Xbox. Microsoft launched a free, ad-supported tier of Xbox Cloud Gaming on November 12 — the same day the outage began. And the cloud is about to matter more than ever: Grand Theft Auto VI arrives November 19, and it will be streamable on Xbox consoles via Xbox Cloud Gaming at launch, including on previous-gen Xbox One hardware. A cloud service players cannot reach is a problem; a cloud service players cannot reach six days before the biggest launch of the year is a bigger one.

What's next

Microsoft says mail is moving again and email queues are shrinking, and it extended its email timeout to 48 hours to protect delayed messages. But capacity has not returned to normal, some users still face intermittent issues, and there is still no ETA for a full fix. If your Xbox still will not sign in, check the status page first — and then check Downdetector, because the two are not telling the same story right now.

Collectibles · Nov 13, 2026 · 9:09 PM ET

Star Trek Commander decks hit shelves today

By Matt

Magic: The Gathering's four Star Trek Commander decks arrive today at $74.99 each — Picard, Spock, Worf and the Borg Queen, each leading a ready-to-play 100-card deck with 32 brand-new cards.

Disclosure: this post contains affiliate links. I earn a commission on qualifying purchases.

The launch

Magic: The Gathering's Star Trek crossover is on shelves today. Four Commander decks arrive November 13, 2026, at $74.99 each, each one ready to play straight out of the box with a foil borderless commander on top.

Every deck is a full 100-card build, and each one carries 32 cards that are entirely new to Magic — the rest is reprints chosen to fit the deck's game plan. Wizards of the Coast revealed the full decklists October 27–29, after a November 6–12 prerelease week at local game stores and a November 10 digital rollout on MTG Arena.

This is the first full-scale Universes Beyond crossover with Star Trek, and it lands in the franchise's 60th anniversary year. The main set is available alongside the decks, from $6.99 Play Boosters up to Collector Boosters, with the Commander decks as the on-ramp for anyone who wants to sit down and play tonight.

The four decks

Each deck is built around a different corner of the Star Trek universe, with its own colors and its own way to win.

Federation Fleet: Jean-Luc Picard; White, blue, red; Draw cards and power up starships

Landing Party: Spock; Green, white, blue; Play lands — especially differently named ones

Klingon Fury: Worf; Red, white, black; Attack every opponent at once

We Are the Borg: The Borg Queen; White, blue, black; Make Borg tokens and steal creatures

Federation Fleet is the Jeskai starship deck, built around Picard and the fleet — card draw feeding bigger ships. Landing Party is the Bant lands deck, with Spock rewarding you for controlling a wide spread of differently named lands through the Strange New Worlds mechanic. Klingon Fury is the Mardu aggro deck, pure Worf: hit the whole table at once. We Are the Borg is the Esper inevitability deck, turning the Assimilate mechanic into a slow takeover as your opponents' creatures become Borg drones.

What's in the box

Beyond the 100 cards and the foil borderless face commander, each deck comes with 10 double-sided token cards, a deck box that holds 100 sleeved cards, a strategy insert and a reference card. The Commander decks are legal in eternal formats like Commander, Legacy and Vintage.

For collectors who want the full treatment, all four decks also come in a Collector's Edition at $159.99 each, with every card in surge foil.

One thing to check before you head out

Wizards has warned that Star Trek Commander decks in North America may arrive after the November 13 release date, thanks to shipping delays on some products. If your local store doesn't have them on the shelf today, keep your preorder and ask the store for its expected date — they may land a few days late rather than not at all.

Commander Party events run November 20–26, with a second wave December 11–17, so stores have organized-play windows stretching into the holiday season either way.

TSL Bets · Nov 13, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

TSL Bets: The Friday parlay (~+264) — Hawks -7.5 at the 1-9 Bulls, Grizzlies -3.5 at the Pelicans

By Matt

Two road favorites against two of the league's worst teams. Atlanta is laying 7.5 in Chicago against a 1-9 Bulls team missing its rookie highlight, and Memphis is laying 3.5 in New Orleans. Two -110 legs, about +264.

The games

Atlanta Hawks (7-5) at Chicago Bulls (1-9) — 8:00 PM ET, United Center, Chicago

Hawks -7.5 (-110), Bulls +7.5 (-110), total 245.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Injuries — Atlanta: Jalen Johnson is back from the left ankle sprain that cost him time — he returned Wednesday against the Wizards. Rookie point guard Kingston Flemings is questionable with a back issue; if he sits, CJ McCollum returns to the starting unit. Asa Newell is out with a toe injury, and Mouhamed Gueye, Keshon Gilbert and Henri Veesaar remain out.

Injuries — Chicago: Rookie Caleb Wilson is out after fracturing his right hand — surgery, four to six weeks, at a moment when he was playing 30.8 minutes a night and serving as the nightly highlight for the franchise. Norman Powell is questionable with a shoulder injury. Tobe Awaka is out with a back injury.

Storylines: The Hawks have won four of five and are rested, kicking off a three-game road trip still annoyed about Monday's 124-114 loss to the Lakers. The Bulls are 1-9 and coming off the worst kind of loss: they shot 85 percent in the first quarter Wednesday, built a 22-point lead against San Antonio — their largest of the season — and lost anyway. This is an NBA Cup group game, Atlanta 1-0 in East Group C, Chicago 0-1. The Hawks are the better team, the healthier team, and the team with something to prove after Monday.

Memphis Grizzlies (7-4) at New Orleans Pelicans (4-8) — 8:00 PM ET, Smoothie King Center, New Orleans

Grizzlies -3.5 (-110), Pelicans +3.5 (-110), total 235.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Injuries — Memphis: Zach Edey is out with an ankle injury. Scotty Pippen Jr. is out with a back injury. Quinten Post is out in concussion protocol.

Injuries — New Orleans: Trendon Watford is out with an ankle injury. Trey Murphy III, Yves Missi, Bennedict Mathurin and Bryce McGowens are all game-time decisions.

Storylines: Memphis is 7-4 and laying only 3.5 on the road against a 4-8 Pelicans team that has half its rotation on the injury report. The Grizzlies are shorthanded up front without Edey, but the Pelicans' list is longer and more important — four game-time decisions on top of an out. This is also an NBA Cup group game, and the number is small enough that Memphis does not need a statement win, just a win.

The bet

2-leg parlay (~+264): Hawks -7.5 (-110) + Grizzlies -3.5 (-110). Two road favorites against two of the league's worst teams, both laying numbers that reflect the gap rather than exaggerating it. Neither leg is a big spread — 7.5 and 3.5 — and both favorites are healthier, in better form, and playing teams whose injury reports read like a roster sheet. The Hawks are catching a Bulls team that just blew a 22-point lead at home and lost its best young player for a month. The Grizzlies are catching a Pelicans team that might not know its starting five until an hour before tip.

The honest caveat

Both legs are road favorites, and road favorites lose games they should win — Chicago is at home, desperate, and the Hawks are on the first night of a road trip with a rookie point guard who might not play. The Memphis leg is the shakier one: the Grizzlies are missing their center and two rotation players, and if Murphy and Missi both suit up for New Orleans, the Pelicans are a different team than the injury report suggests. A parlay needs both, and the second leg is the one that keeps this honest.

The pick

Hawks -7.5 + Grizzlies -3.5, about +264. A $10 ticket returns about $36.40. Two favorites, two bad teams, two small numbers.

TSL Bets · Nov 13, 2026 · 8:01 AM ET

TSL Bets: Rangers -1.5 (+114) at Canucks — the worst team in the league, and a plus-money puck line

By Matt

The Rangers are 11-8-1 and rolling into Vancouver, where the 3-16-1 Canucks are starting a .889 goalie. The puck line pays plus money. Take it.

The game

New York Rangers (11-8-1) at Vancouver Canucks (3-16-1) — 9:00 PM ET, Rogers Arena, Vancouver, ESPN+/MSGSN

Rangers -1.5 (+114), Canucks +1.5 (-135), Rangers moneyline -225, Canucks +185, total 6.5 (DraftKings via ESPN).

Goalies: Dylan Garand is expected to start for New York — 12 starts this season, 6-7-1, .905 save percentage, 2.48 goals-against average, one shutout. He has been the lead netminder since Igor Shesterkin went down with a season-ending injury six games into the year, and the Rangers have now alternated him with a returning Joonas Korpisalo, who backstopped Wednesday's 3-1 win in Calgary. Leevi Merilainen is expected for Vancouver — 2-8-1 with a .889 save percentage.

Injuries — New York: Shesterkin is out for the season. Korpisalo is back from the foot infection that sidelined him to start the year. Sean Durzi is unlikely to play after practicing in a red non-contact jersey on Thursday.

Injuries — Vancouver: Thatcher Demko is out with a hip injury. Filip Chytil is out with an upper-body injury. Tom Willander is day-to-day with a lower-body injury. Akil Thomas and Mackenzie MacEachern are out. The Canucks claimed defenseman Cameron Crotty off waivers on Wednesday.

Storylines: The gap between these teams is the whole bet. The Rangers have won two of three on their Pacific road trip, Mika Zibanejad has two goals and four assists in his last five games, Adam Fox leads the team with 22 points and Alexis Lafreniere leads it with nine goals. The Rangers are 11-1-0 when they score first and 0-7-1 when they give up the first goal — so the first goal matters more than usual here. Vancouver is 3-16-1, the worst record in the league, and its top performers over the last five games are Elias Pettersson with four assists and no goals, and Jonathan Lekkerimaki with three points. The Canucks' leading goal scorers, Brock Boeser and Lekkerimaki, have seven each — and the team has won three games all season.

The bet

Rangers -1.5 (+114). The moneyline at -225 is unplayable chalk, and the puck line is where the value lives: plus money on a good team against the worst team in the league, with a .905 goalie against a .889 one. The Rangers do not need a blowout — they need a two-goal win against a team that has managed three wins in twenty games. Vancouver has lost the kind of games where two goals is a lot to ask of the favorite, but the Canucks are not losing close — they are 3-16-1 for a reason, and the reason is in net and on the scoresheet.

The honest caveat

The Rangers are on the road at the end of a trip, and Garand is a rookie carrying a starter's load because the team's number one is done for the year. Vancouver is at home, closing a homestand, and bad teams win games they should not — the Canucks only need one good night from Merilainen and a first goal to flip the whole script, since the Rangers are 0-7-1 when they concede first. The puck line also needs a two-goal margin, and a 2-1 or 3-2 Rangers win still loses the bet. This is a bet on the gap being real, and the gap is real — but a one-goal game is always one bounce away.

The pick

Rangers -1.5 (+114). A $10 ticket returns about $21.40. The worst team in the league, a plus-money puck line, and a goalie mismatch.

Collectibles · Nov 13, 2026 · 8:00 AM ET

Delta Reign singles are spiking while Star Trek crashes on release day — this week's TCG market movers

By Matt

Pokémon's Delta Reign is a week old and its singles are climbing — Aarune up 120% in five days — while Magic's Star Trek set hits paper today with its priciest foils already down 80% from their pre-release highs.

The movers

A week into Pokémon's Mega Evolution — Delta Reign, the singles market is doing what it always does with a new set: sorting the real chases from the launch-week noise. Aarune, a holofoil at #139/103, is the week's biggest climber across every set tracked — $25.36, up from $11.52 on November 7, a 120 percent jump in five days. Kecleon, holofoil #117/103, is up 32 percent to $31.02. The sealed side is moving the other way: the Pokémon Center-exclusive Delta Reign Elite Trainer Box is down 27 percent to $152.16 from $208.63, as more boxes hit the market and the early premium bleeds off.

Magic's Star Trek set is the mirror image — and it lands on paper today. The priciest foils have already crashed from their pre-release highs: Mr. Spock, Science Officer in borderless foil is down 82 percent to $66.12 from $358.79, and General Picard, Earth Warlord in borderless foil is down 80 percent to $49 from $250. That is the normal release-week pattern — prices fall as more boxes get opened — but the scale of the drop says the pre-release market got way ahead of itself. The climbers in the set are the cheaper foils: Deep Space 9 foil is up 113 percent to $22.08, and Starbase Yorktown borderless is up 107 percent to $17.90.

The old guard is still moving, too. Sun & Moon Prism Star cards top TCGplayer's latest 30-day risers, led by Giratina ◇ from Ultra Prism — up $52.79 to $92.43 — and Base Set Unlimited Charizard is on the same list at $987.13. In the 30th Celebration set, the Mew G/RGB holofoil is up 9 percent to $3,456.93.

Release day, with a catch

Magic: The Gathering | Star Trek hits tabletop today, November 13, three days after its Arena debut. But not everything is arriving on time: Wizards says several North American products are delayed, including Commander decks, and some EMEA, Asia-Pacific and New Zealand products may also miss the launch date. Play Boosters and Prerelease Packs are on schedule — prereleases ran November 6 through 12 — so the set is playable today even where the Commander decks are late.

One more date for the calendar: Riftbound bans are announced tomorrow, November 14.

What's next

Delta Reign's singles will keep sorting themselves out over the next two weeks, and Star Trek's prices will settle once the delayed products actually land. The pattern to watch is the same one every release shows: the cards people are actually playing climb, the ones people were speculating on fall. Aarune is the first test of which kind it is.

Gaming · Nov 13, 2026 · 8:00 AM ET

Rockstar shared a GTA 6 clip so real-looking that fans are arguing about whether it is the game at all

By Matt

Rockstar shared a 15-second GTA 6 clip recreating a real Miami street scene in Leonida, and fans cannot agree whether they are watching gameplay, real life, or AI. The game launches November 19.

The clip

Six days before launch, Rockstar Games shared a 15-second GTA 6 clip that has the internet arguing about what it is actually looking at.

The clip started with Only in Dade, the Instagram account that posts unusual incidents from Miami-Dade County, and shows a street full of customized sports cars with people filming on their phones. Rockstar then shared it, recreating the same scene inside Leonida — the game's version of Florida — side by side with the real Miami footage the studio shot in October. The recreation is so close that viewers are debating whether they are watching actual gameplay, real-life video, or something AI-generated. Only in Dade described it as 100 percent gameplay footage, and Rockstar sharing it is the tell: this is the studio showing off its graphics, not passing off a fake video as real.

Look closely and the seams show, barely. The real clip has more people walking around; the game version has fewer pedestrians. There is also first-person footage in the comparison, which answers one open question — first-person view exists in the game for at least some of it — while leaving another open: whether the whole game can be played that way, or only while walking around and driving. November 19 is when that answer arrives.

The launch

The timing is deliberate. This is the final week of the longest marketing runway in gaming history, and Rockstar has moved from silence into a tight rollout: the 90-second launch trailer in October, pre-loading open since Tuesday, and now a clip that does not ask anyone to trust a claim about realism on faith — it shows the reference and the recreation together.

The game launches November 19 on PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series X|S, with no PC version announced. IShowSpeed has reportedly said he plans to travel to New Zealand to stream his first gameplay session at midnight local time on launch day, though that plan has not been officially confirmed. YouTube will run a 24-hour launch stream from midnight ET on November 19, with live gameplay and creator shows.

Entertainment · Nov 13, 2026 · 7:59 AM ET

IShowSpeed three-peats as Streamer of the Year — Kai Cenat matches him with three awards of his own

By Matt

IShowSpeed won Streamer of the Year for the third straight time at the sixth annual Streamer Awards in Los Angeles, taking three trophies. Kai Cenat matched him with three, Jynxzi took Gamer of the Year, and Quackity received the Legacy Award.

The night

The sixth annual Streamer Awards took place Thursday night in Los Angeles, hosted by QTCinderella and Caroline Kwan — moved up a week from November 17 so it would not collide with the launch of Grand Theft Auto VI — and the two biggest names in streaming split the night right down the middle.

IShowSpeed won Streamer of the Year for the third consecutive time, beating CaseOh, Joe Bartolozzi, Jynxzi and Stable Ronaldo. He took three awards in all: the big one, Best IRL Streamer, and Best Streamed Series for his World Cup Tour. Kai Cenat matched him trophy for trophy with three of his own: League of Their Own, Best Streamed Event for Streamer University, and Best Mainstream Collab for his stream with singer Tyla.

Jynxzi was named Gamer of the Year, beating Feinberg, iiTzTimmy, supertf and TheBurntPeanut. CaseOh took Best Variety Streamer. Katie B and Vanillamace each won three as well — Katie B for Best Breakout Streamer, Best Minecraft Streamer and Best Stream Duo with Vanillamace; Vanillamace for the Sapphire Award, Best Dressed and that same duo award. Quackity received the Legacy Award. Lacy won the new Biggest Crashout award, E11ysa took Rising Star, and ashswag beat Peller and Carter Efe for Best International Streamer.

The show around the show

Thirty-seven categories were decided by a mix of fan voting — 70 percent — and an industry panel for the rest. The night also had the strangest subplot of awards season: the show's trophies were stolen from the front porch weeks before the ceremony, and the show went on anyway. For the first time, Kick streamers were eligible for nominations.

Speed's three-peat is the headline, but the balance of the night is the story — four creators walked out with three awards each, and the two men at the top of the platform split the biggest prizes between them. Nobody ran away with the show. The show is the point.

What's next

The next date on streaming's calendar is not an awards show — it is November 19, when GTA 6 launches and half the creators in that room will be live playing it. Speed has already said he plans to stream his first session from New Zealand at midnight local time.
